Geometric Langlands in positive characteristic from characteristic zero
Dennis Gaitsgory and Sam Raskin

Abstract
. We establish part of the statement of the geometric Langlands conjecture for ℓ \ell -adic sheaves over a field of positive characteristic. Namely, we show that the category of automorphic sheaves with nilpotent singular support is equivalent to the appropriately defined category of ind-coherent sheaves on the union of some of the connected components of the stack of Langlands parameters.



Introduction

0.1.
What is done in this paper?

0.1.1.

This paper can be considered as a sequel to both the [AGKRRV] and [GLC] series. Namely,
in
[
AGKRRV1
, Conjecture 21.2.7]
we proposed a version of the geometric Langlands conjecture
(GLC) that makes sense in the context of
\ell
-adic sheaves (for curves over a field of any characteristic).

Namely, it says that the (derived) category

(0.1)

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

of
\ell
-adic sheaves on the moduli stack
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
of principal
G
-bundles on a (smooth and complete)
curve
X
,
with nilpotent singular support
, is equivalent to the category

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}),

where:

•

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
is the prestack of
{\check{G}}
-local systems on
X

with restricted variation
,
introduced in
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 1.4]
;

•

The subscript “Nilp” stands for the restriction on the singular support (as coherent sheaves), introduced in
[
AG1
, Sect. 11.1]
.

Remark 0.1.2
.

The fact that the subcategory (
0.1
) inside the ambient
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is the “right object to consider” as far as automorphic sheaves
are concerned was a discovery of G. Laumon in his seminal paper
[
Laum
]
.

There he conjectured that all Hecke eigensheaves must belong to this subcategory. This conjecture
was settled in
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 14.4.3]
. In fact, more is true: in
loc. cit.
, Theorem 14.4.4
it was shown that “any object of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
that remotely looks like a Hecke eigensheaf” belongs to

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

0.1.3.

Unfortunately, we still cannot prove the full GLC over a field of positive characteristic. Rather, in this paper we establish a partial result.
We construct a functor

(0.2)

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}),

and we prove.

Theorem 0.1.4
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}
factors via an equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\sim}{\to}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\subset{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}),

where
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
is the union of some of the connected components of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
.

This is Theorem
1.3.9
(i) in the main body of the paper.

Remark 0.1.5
.

We remind the reader that the connected components of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
correspond bijectively to

semi-simple

{\check{G}}
-local systems (two local systems lie in the same connected component if
and only if they have isomorphic semi-simplifications).

In particular, every irreducible
{\check{G}}
-local system lies in its own connected component (which is, however,
stacky and non-reduced).

0.1.6.

In addition, we prove:

Theorem 0.1.7
.

If
G=GL_{n}
, then the inclusion

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}\subset\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}

is an equality.

So at least for
G=GL_{n}
, Laumon’s vision for the structure of what he called “geometric Langlands correspondence”
has been fully realized.

Remark 0.1.8
.

One can say that for an arbitrary group
G
, we have not solved the most mysterious part of the
Langlands conjecture: we do not know that to an irreducible
{\check{G}}
-local system there corresponds
a non-zero Hecke eigensheaf.

Over fields of characteristic
0
we know this thanks to the Beilinson-Drinfeld construction
of eigensheaves for D-modules, using localization of modules over the affine Kac-Moody algebra at
the critical level
1
1

1

Formally speaking, this is not how the proof of GLC for D-modules given in

[
GLC5
]
proceeds; however, it does crucially rely on the localization of KM-modules, albeit slightly
differently.
.

0.1.9.
Assumptions on the characteristic

The above results rely on the validity of
[
AGKRRV1
]
, for which certain assumptions on the
characteristic of the ground field are needed, see Sects. 14.4.1 and D.1.1 in
loc. cit.

0.2.
Function-theoretic applications

The equivalence established in Theorem
0.1.4
allows us to deduce information about the classical theory
of automorphic functions (in the unramified case over function fields).

0.2.1.

Namely, we let our ground field
k
be the algebraic closure of a finite field
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, and we assume that both

X
and
G
are defined over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. In this case, the geometric objects involved in (
0.2
)
carry an automorphism, given by the action of Frobenius.

Taking its categorical trace and applying some existing calculations (namely, ones from
[
AGKRRV3
]
and
[
BLR
]
),
from Theorem
0.1.4
we obtain:

Theorem 0.2.2
.

There exists an identification of vector spaces

(0.3)

\operatorname{Funct}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\simeq\Gamma({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},\omega),

where:

•

\operatorname{Funct}_{c}(-,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})
stands for the space of compactly supported functions;

•

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}:=(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})^{\operatorname{Frob}}
is the moduli space of Weil local systems on
X
with respect to
{\check{G}}
,
and
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}:=({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})^{\operatorname{Frob}}
;

•

\omega
is the dualizing sheaf.

This is stated as Corollary
1.5.6
in the main body of the paper.

Moreover, it follows from the construction of the isomorphism
(
0.3
) that it is compatible with the action of the excursion algebra

{\mathcal{A}}_{G}:=\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},{\mathcal{O}})

on both sides, and in particular, with the action of the Hecke operators.

Remark 0.2.3
.

Parallel to Remark
0.1.8
, Theorem
0.2.2
does not settle the main mystery in classical Langlands:
outside the case of
G=GL_{n}
, we do not yet know that to an irreducible Langlands parameter there
corresponds a non-zero eigenfunction (if it existed, it would automatically be cuspidal, by the nature of
the isomorphism (
0.3
)).

Remark 0.2.4
.

The stack
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}
is Calabi-Yau
2
2

2

In the sense that the determinant of the cotangent complex
is the trivial line bundle.
, but is also highly derived in that its structure sheaf has
non-trivial cohomology in infinitely many negative cohomological degrees. This implies that although there
is a canonical map

(0.4)

{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}}\to\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}},

it is very far from being an isomorphism (although it is such over the
quasi-smooth
locus).

Ultimately, this phenomenon is responsible for the presence of the Arthur
SL_{2}
in the
classification of automorphic functions.

Yet, we expect that in the map

(0.5)

\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}})\to\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}}),

induced by (
0.4
), both sides are classical vector spaces (i.e., are concentrated in cohomological degree
0
)
and the map (
0.5
) itself is injective. Moreover, we expect that
the intersection of the image of (
0.5
) with the subspace of cuspidal
functions is equal to the space of
tempered
cuspidal functions
(with respect to any isomorphism
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}
). We hope to take this up in a future work.

Remark 0.2.5
.

The paper
[
Ra2
]
proves an arithmetic result —
the non-existence of cusp forms with certain Langlands parameters —
conditional on GLC in
characteristic
p
.
Although we only obtain partial results on GLC in
this paper, our results suffice for the
applications in
[
Ra2
]
.

0.3.
The methods

0.3.1.

The first step is the construction of the functor (
0.2
).
It is here that the significance of the subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

comes to the fore.

Namely, it turns out to be the maximal subcategory of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
on which the action
of the Hecke functors factors through a monoidal action of the category

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}});

we refer to it as the “spectral action”.

We recover the functor (
0.2
)
by requiring that it intertwines the actions of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
on the two sides
and makes the following diagram commute:

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Vect}@>{\operatorname{Id}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@A{\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}}A{}A@A{}A{\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}),\end{CD}

where the left vertical arrow is the functor of
vacuum
Whittaker coefficient,
see Sect.
9.3.1
.

This follows verbatim the construction of the Langlands functor for D-modules and Betti sheaves
in
[
GLC1
, Sect. 1]
.

0.3.2.

As a next step, we show that the functor (
0.2
) is an equivalence
for
\ell
-adic sheaves as long as the ground field over which we work has characteristic
0
.

To do so, by the Lefschetz principle, we can replace the initial ground field by the field
{\mathbb{C}}
of complex
numbers. In the latter case, we compare the functor (
0.2
) for
\ell
-adic
sheaves with its counterpart for Betti sheaves, and we show that if the latter is an equivalence, then
so is the
\ell
-adic version.

Finally, we quote
[
GLC1
]
, which says that the Betti version of (
0.2
)

is
an equivalence. This is obtained by combining the fact that the D-module version of
(
0.2
) is an equivalence (which is the outcome of the [GLC] series)
and the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence.

0.3.3.

Thus, our task is to deduce Theorem
0.1.4
for a field of positive characteristic
from its validity for a field of characteristic
0
. We achieve this by the following procedure.

Let
{\mathsf{k}}
be our ground field of positive characteristic (assumed algebraically closed).
Let
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}:=\operatorname{Witt}({\mathsf{k}})
be the ring of Witt vectors of
{\mathsf{k}}
, let
{\mathsf{K}}_{0}
denote the field of fractions of
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}
and let

{\mathsf{K}}
denote the algebraic closure of
{\mathsf{K}}_{0}
. Let
{\mathsf{R}}
denote the integral closure of
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}

in
{\mathsf{K}}
.

Given a (smooth complete) curve
X_{\mathsf{k}}
over
{\mathsf{k}}
, we
choose
its extension to a (smooth complete) curve

X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
. (Such an extension exists
by a standard deformation theory argument.) Let
X_{\mathsf{K}}
be the base change of
X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
to
K
.

Note that we can identify
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}
with the union of some of the connected components
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{K}}}
, see Sect.
3.1.3
.

0.3.4.

In Sect.
3
we introduce a specialization functor

(0.6)

\operatorname{Sp}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}),

which essentially amounts to the functor of nearby cycles.

We establish the following properties of this functor:

•

It commutes with the Hecke functors;

•

It commutes with the functors of Eisenstein series;

•

It sends the vacuum Poincaré object
3
3

3

This is the object that corepresents the functor
\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}
.

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{K}}}
to the vacuum Poincaré object
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{k}}}
.

The first of these properties implies that the functor
\operatorname{Sp}
sends the direct summand

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})

that has to do with
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}\subset\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{K}}}
to

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
, i.e., we obtain a functor

(0.7)

\operatorname{Sp}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}).

Combined with the other properties, one shows that the functor (
0.7
) preserves compactness
and makes the following diagram commute:

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})@>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{K}}}>{\sim}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}}.{\mathsf{k}}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Sp}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Id}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})@>{}>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{k}}}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}}.{\mathsf{k}}}).\end{CD}

0.3.5.

However, this is not quite enough to deduce Theorem
0.1.4
. What we need is another
crucial property of the functor (
0.7
), which says that this functor is a
Verdier quotient
.

It turns out that there is a simple criterion for when a functor between dualizable categories
is a Verdier quotient, see Lemma
5.4.5
. This is a general categorical assertion, but
it turns out that one can apply and check it in our situation; this is due to some rather
special properties of the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
established in
[
AGKRRV2
]
,
most notably, the categorical Künneth formula.

The fact that this criterion is satisfied for us follows from a certain geometric property of the initial functor
(
0.6
). Namely, this property says that the natural map

(0.8)

(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})\to\operatorname{Sp}((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})),

is an isomorphism, where:

•

\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}
denotes the diagonal morphism of a stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
;

•

\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
denotes the constant sheaf on
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

In its turn, the fact that (
0.8
) is an isomorphism is equivalent to the ULA property of

(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})
with respect to the projection

\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\to\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

We verify the required ULA property using the Drinfeld-Lafforgue-Vinberg compactification
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}
of the diagonal
map of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

0.3.6.
Another technical result

We now mention another result, Theorem
1.1.7
, established in this paper,
which is a crucial technical component for many other theorems that we
prove.

Namely, Theorem
1.1.7
says that the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is
generated by objects that are compact in the ambient category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

This result was conjectured in
[
AGKRRV1
]
4
4

4

This was used also as a hypothesis in
[
AGKRRV3
]

to show that the trace isomorphism
\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}))\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})

reproduces the usual pointwise Frobenius map for constructible Weil sheaves.
, and it was proved in
loc. cit.

when the ground field has characteristic
0
. In this paper we deduce it
in the positive characteristic case using the functor (
0.7
).

0.4.
Structure of the paper

0.4.1.

In Sect.
1
we construct the Langlands functor (
0.2
)
and derive function-theoretic applications.

0.4.2.

In Sect.
2
we prove that the functor (
0.2
)
is an equivalence over a ground field of characteristic
0
. Namely, we deduce
this from the validity of the Betti version of GLC.

0.4.3.

In Sect.
3
we stipulate the existence of the functor (
0.6
) with
some specified properties and deduce Theorem
0.1.4
.

0.4.4.

In Sect.
4
we construct the functor (
0.6
) and establish some
of its expected properties.

0.4.5.

In Sect.
5
we state Theorem
5.1.3
, which says that
(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})

is ULA and show how this implies that the functor (
0.7
) is a Verdier quotient.

0.4.6.

In Sect.
6
we prove the local acyclicity theorems responsible for the required
properties of the functor (
0.6
). There are four such theorems:

(i) Acyclicity of kernels defining Hecke functors. This is proved
using (what essentially is) the Bott-Samelson resolution;

(ii) Acyclicity of kernels defining Eisenstein functors. This is proved
using local models, called
Zastava
spaces.

(iii) Acyclicity of
(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})
.
This is proved using local models for
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}
, developed in
[
Sch
]
.

(iv) Acyclicity of the vacuum Poincaré object
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
.

In the present section we prove the first three of these theorems. The proofs are based on the

contraction principle
, formulated in Proposition
6.2.2
.

0.4.7.

In Sect.
7
we prove the acyclicity of the vacuum Poincaré object. We give two proofs.
One is shorter, but it uses an additional assumption on the interaction of the characteristic of the field with
g(X)
and
G
.

The second proof uses a comparison between the !-Poincaré object
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!}

with its *-counterpart
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{*}
, expressed by Theorem
7.5.4
.

0.4.8.

In Sect.
8
we prove Theorem
7.5.4
, which says that the cone of the natural map

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!}\to\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{*}

belongs to the full subcategory generated by the essential images of the Eisenstein functors for
proper parabolic subgroups.

Theorem
7.5.4
is of independent interest, and the proof that we give is useful as well:
it consists of studying what one may call the asymptotic behavior of the Whttaker sheaf
as we degenerate the character.

0.4.9.

Finally, in Sect.
9
we revisit the topic of the interaction of the Langlands functor
with Eisenstein series.

So far we have not mentioned Eisenstein series in the introduction, but not surprisingly,
they form an integral part of the theory, and our ability to prove statements about the
Langlands correspondence often relies on having good control of the Eisenstein
functor.

One particular aspect of this is the computation of the Whittaker coefficient
of Eisenstein series, which is performed in Theorem
9.2.7
.

0.4.10.
Notations and conventions

Notations and conventions in this paper are identical to those in
[
GLC1
]
.

0.5.
Acknowledgements

We dedicate this paper to Gérard Laumon, who initiated the study of automorphic sheaves.
In addition to that, the influence of his ideas in this area is all-pervasive: geometric Eisenstein
series, geometric Fourier transform, local Fourier-Mukai equivalence, to name just a few.

We are grateful to our collaborators on the [AGKRRV] and [GLC] projects:
D. Arinkin, D. Beraldo, J. Campbell, L. Chen, J. Faergeman, D. Kazhdan, K. Lin, N. Rozenblyum
and Y. Varshavsky.

We are also grateful to D. Ben-Zvi, T. Feng, D. Hansen, M. Harris, V. Lafforgue, W. Sawin, P. Scholze and X. Zhu
for productive discussions.

This paper was written while the first author was visiting IHES and the second author was visiting the
Max Planck Institute in Bonn. We wish to thank these institutions for providing excellent working conditions.

The work of the second author was supported by the Sloan Research Fellowship, as well as
NSF grant DMS-2401526.

1.
The Langlands functor for
\ell
-adic sheaves

In this section we will work over a ground field
k
, assumed algebraically closed,
but it may have either positive characteristic or characteristic
0
. Our sheaf
theory
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
(see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 1.1]
for what we mean by that)
will be that of (ind-)constructible
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-adic étale sheaves;
so our field of coefficients
\mathsf{e}
is
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
.

On the geometric side, we consider the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, as defined
in
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 14.1]
.

On the spectral side we, we consider the category
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
,
as defined in
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 4.1]
.

The goal of this section is to construct a functor

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{restr}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})

and state some results and conjectures pertaining to its properties.

1.1.
Coarse version of the functor

1.1.1.

We start by considering the object

\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}.

It is constructed by the procedure of
[
GLC1
, Sect. 3.3]
(see Sect.
7.1.1
below).

Remark 1.1.2
.

Note that when
k
has positive characteristic,
\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}}
can be equivalently
constructed by the procedure of
[
GLC1
, Sect. 1.3.7]
, replacing the exponential D-module by the
Artin-Schreier sheaf, see
[
GLC1
, Remark 3.3.6]
.

1.1.3.

Recall the functor

{\mathsf{P}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

of
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 15.4.5]
.

Denote:

\operatorname{Poinc}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}}}^{\operatorname{Vac}}:={\mathsf{P}}(\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

1.1.4.

Recall now that, according to
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 14.3.2]
, the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is
acted on by
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
.

We define the functor

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

to be given by the action of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
on
\operatorname{Poinc}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}}}^{\operatorname{Vac}}
.

We will prove:

Proposition 1.1.5
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}
preserves compactness.

The proof will be given in Sect.
1.6
.

1.1.6.

As stated, Proposition
1.1.5
says that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}
sends compacts
to compacts, when viewed as a functor with values in
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
. The following assertion was
stated as
[
AGKRRV1
, Conjecture 14.1.8]
; we will prove it in this paper (see Sect.
3.1.12
):

Theorem 1.1.7
.

The embedding

\operatorname{emb.Nilp}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

preserves compactness.

Thus, Proposition
1.1.5
, combined with Theorem
1.1.7
, say that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}
,
when viewed as taking values in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, also preserves compactness.

1.1.8.

By Proposition
1.1.5
, the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}
admits a continuous
right adjoint, which we will denote by
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}
is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear by construction.
Hence, the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}
acquires a structure of right-lax linearity with respect
to
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
.

Note, however, since
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
is
semi-rigid
as a symmetric monoidal category (see
[
AGKRRV1
, Appendix C]

for what this means), we obtain that the right-lax linear structure on
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}
is actually strict.

1.1.9.

We will prove:

Theorem 1.1.10
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}
sends compact objects in
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

to bounded below (a.k.a. eventually coconnective) objects in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
.

The proof of this theorem will be given in Sect.
1.7
.

1.2.
The Whittaker coefficient functor

1.2.1.

Let

\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect}

be the functor co-represented by
\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}}
.

When
\operatorname{char}(k)
is positive, it is given by (
9.6
).
A variant of this holds when
\operatorname{char}(k)=0
using the material in
[
GLC1
, Sect. 3.3]
.

1.2.2.

Recall the functor

\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-):{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\to\operatorname{Vect},

see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 7.7]
.

Remark 1.2.3
.

Explicitly, the functor
\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)
fits into the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})@>{(\Upsilon_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}})^{\vee}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{\Gamma^{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)}V{}V@V{}V{\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)}V\\
\operatorname{Vect}@>{\operatorname{Id}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}.\end{CD}

This diagram is valid for any laft formal algebraic stack. Note, however, that for quasi-smooth
5
5

5

In fact,
an appropriate eventual connectivity assumption suffices.

formal algebraic stacks (such as
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
), the top horizontal arrow is a Verdier
quotient.

1.2.4.

We will prove:

Proposition 1.2.5
.

The composition

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

identifies canonically with

(1.1)

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{emb.Nilp}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

The proposition will be proved in Sect.
1.6.7
.

Remark 1.2.6
.

Recall (see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 7.6.1]
) that
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
is canonically self-dual, so that under this sel-fduality the object

{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
corresponds to the functor
\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)
.
In particular, a
{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}
-linear functor

{\mathbf{C}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})

(for a
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear category
{\mathbf{C}}
) is uniquely recovered from the composition

{\mathbf{C}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

From here here we obtain that Proposition
1.2.5
gives rise to the following characterization of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}
:
it is the unique functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})

that satisfies:

•

It is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear;

•

Its composition with
\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)
is isomorphic to (
1.1
).

1.3.
Construction of the Langlands functor

1.3.1.

Let
{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec}}
denote the functor

(1.2)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\Upsilon_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}}{\simeq}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\{0\}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\overset{-\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}}{\simeq}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\{0\}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\hookrightarrow\\
\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}),

where:

•

The first arrow is given by tensoring by the dualizing sheaf
\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}
;
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
;

•

{\mathfrak{l}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}
is the graded line bundle
6
6

6

In fact, this line bundle is constant, i.e.,
is essentially a graded line over
\mathsf{e}
, see Remark
1.3.3
below.

\det(T^{*}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})^{-1})[-2(g-1)\dim(G)]
.

In what follows we will denote the composition of the first two arrows in (
1.2
) by
\Xi_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}
.
This is a functor that makes sense for any quasi-smooth formal scheme (resp., algebrac stack)
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
If
{\mathcal{Z}}
is an
actual scheme (resp., algebraic stack), then
\Xi_{\mathcal{Z}}
is the tautological functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}})\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}),

whose essential image is
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\{0\}}({\mathcal{Z}})
.

Remark 1.3.2
.

We use the notation
{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}
, rather than the more common one, namely,

\Psi_{{\operatorname{Nilp}},\{0\}}
, in order to avoid the clash with the symbol for the nearby cycles functor.

Remark 1.3.3
.

The second arrow in (
1.2
) is introduced in order to make this functor compatible with the one in the de Rham and Betti versions.
Note also that
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
is symplectic (the symplectic structure is constructed using a choice of an invariant form on
{\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
)
of dimension
[2(g-1)\dim(G)]
. Hence, the line bundle
\det(T^{*}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}))
is canonically constant.

1.3.4.

The functor
{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec}}
preserves compactness and is fully faithful. Let
{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}

denote its right adjoint.

The functor
{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec}}
is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear by construction. Hence, the functor

{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}
acquires a right-lax linear structure. By the semi-rigidity of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
, this right-lax structure is actually
strict.

1.3.5.

From Theorem
1.1.10
, as in
[
GLC1
, Corollary 1.6.5]
, we obtain:

Corollary 1.3.6
.

There exists a continuous functor

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}),

uniquely characterized by the following properties:

(i) The functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}
sends compact objects in
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

to eventually coconnective objects in
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
, i.e., to
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})^{>-\infty}
;

(ii)
({\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec}})^{R}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}\simeq{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}
.

Furthermore, as in
[
GLC1
, Proposition 1.7.2]
, we obtain:

Lemma 1.3.7
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}
carries a unique
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear structure, so that
the induced
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear structure on
({\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec}})^{R}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}
is
the natural
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear structure on
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}
.

1.3.8.

We are now ready to state the main result of this paper:

Main Theorem 1.3.9
.

(i)
The functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}
factors via an equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\sim}{\to}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})

where
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
is the union of some of the connected components of

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
.

(ii) If
\operatorname{char}(k)=0
, then the inclusion
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}\subset\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
is an equality.

(iii) For any
k
and
G=GL_{n}
, the inclusion
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}\subset\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
is an equality.

Of course, we believe that the statement of Theorem
1.3.9
(i) can be strengthened:

Conjecture 1.3.10
.

The inclusion
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}\subset\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
is always as equality.

1.4.
Langlands functor and Eisenstein series

1.4.1.

Let
P^{-}
be a standard (negative) parabolic in
G
and let
M
be its Levi quotient. Consider the Eisenstein
functor

{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

see
[
GLC3
, Sect. 8.1]
.

Note that according to the conventions of
[
GLC3
, Sect. 8.1.3]
, the definition of
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}

includes a cohomological shift, see (
9.7
).

We claim:

Proposition 1.4.2
.

The functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}
sends
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Remark 1.4.3
.

The proof given below uses a spectral description of the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.
One can, however, give a purely geometric argument proving Proposition
1.4.2
:
Namely, one can estimate the singular support of objects of the form
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}({\mathcal{F}})
using
the following assertion:

The singular support of
\jmath_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})

is contained in the union of the conormal of the strata (for the natural stratification of

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}
).

This assertion can be proved using Zastava spaces in a way similar to the manipulation
involved in the proof of Theorem
4.4.5
below.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be a prestack over
\mathsf{e}
mapping to
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}}
. Let

\operatorname{Hecke}({\mathcal{Z}},\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M}))

be the corresponding category of Hecke eigensheaves, see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 15.2]
.

Recall that according to
[
BG2
]
(the case of
P=B)
and its generalization to an arbitrary parabolic in

[
FH
, Theorem 1.6.5.2]
, the functor

(1.3)

\operatorname{Hecke}({\mathcal{Z}},\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M}))\overset{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Hecke}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

factors canonically as

(1.4)

\operatorname{Hecke}({\mathcal{Z}},\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M}))\overset{({\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}})^{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Hecke}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}}\underset{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}},\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M}))\overset{\operatorname{Hecke}({\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!})}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\operatorname{Hecke}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}}\underset{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}},\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}))\overset{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Hecke}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}}\underset{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{({\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Take
{\mathcal{Z}}=\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}}
, and recall that in this case the composition

(1.5)

\operatorname{Hecke}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}},\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M}))\overset{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Hecke}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\overset{\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}},-)\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})

is fully faithful with essential image
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})
(see
[
AGKRRV1
, Proposition 15.5.3(a)]
).

Under the identification (
1.5
), the original functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}
identifies with the
composition of (
1.4
) with

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}},-)\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

The desired assertion follows now from the fact that for any
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}
, the inclusion

\operatorname{Hecke}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime},\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}))\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Hecke}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime},\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}))

is an equality, see again
[
AGKRRV1
, Proposition 15.5.3(a)]
.

∎

1.4.4.

Consider the diagram

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}\overset{{\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}}{\longleftarrow}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}}\overset{{\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}}},

where we note that the morphism
{\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}
is a relative algebraic stack and is quasi-smooth.

The spectral Eisenstein functor

{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}).

is defined to be

({\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}})^{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{*}\circ({\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}},*}.

As in
[
AG1
, Proposition 13.2.6]
, one shows that the functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}
sends

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}).

1.4.5.

In Sect.
9
we will prove:

Theorem 1.4.6
.

The diagram

(1.6)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})@>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{M}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})\\
@V{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}[\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}]}V{}V@V{}V{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{}>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\end{CD}

commutes, where:

•

The functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}
is the precomposition of
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}
with the translation functor
by
\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})\in\operatorname{Bun}_{Z_{M}}
;

•

\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}=\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}})
, see
[
GLC3
, Theorem 10.1.2]
.

1.5.
Consequences for the classical theory of automorphic functions

1.5.1.

We now specialize to the case when
k=\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, but
X
and
G
(and hence also
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
)
are defined over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. The stack
\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}
carries an automorphism induced by
the geometric Frobenius on
X
.

The following assertion was stated in
[
AGKRRV1
]
as a corollary of
loc. cit.
, Conjecture 24.6.9, and
the latter was proved in
[
BLR
, Sect. 6.4.13]
:

Theorem 1.5.2
.

The inclusion

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})

induces an
isomorphism

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}))\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})).

1.5.3.

A standard computation implies that

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}))\simeq\Gamma^{\operatorname{IndCoh}}((\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})^{\operatorname{Frob}},\omega_{(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})^{\operatorname{Frob}}}).

Recall the notation

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}:=(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})^{\operatorname{Frob}}.

According to
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 24.1.4]
,
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}
is a quasi-compact algebraic stack locally
almost of finite type.

Hence, Theorem
1.5.2
implies that we have a canonical isomorphism

(1.7)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}))\simeq\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}}),

where by a slight abuse of notation, we denote by the same symbol

\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}})

the image of

\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}}\in{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}})

under the functor

(\Upsilon_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}})^{\vee}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}).

1.5.4.

Let

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}\subset\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}

be as in Theorem
1.3.9
(i). This is a
{\operatorname{Frob}}
-invariant substack. Set

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}:=({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})^{\operatorname{Frob}}.

It formally follows from Theorem
1.5.2
that we have

(1.8)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}))\simeq\Gamma({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},\omega_{{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}}).

1.5.5.

Combining isomorphism (
1.8
) with Theorem
1.3.9
(i) and
[
AGKRRV3
, Theorem 0.2.6]
, we obtain:

Corollary 1.5.6
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{Funct}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\simeq\Gamma({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},\omega_{{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}}).

1.5.7.

From Corollary
1.5.6
we obtain the following:

Corollary 1.5.8
.

Let
G
be semi-simple, and let
\sigma
be an irreducible Weil
{\check{G}}
-local system. Then the space of automorphic
functions on which the algebra of excursion operators acts by the character corresponding
to
\sigma
is at most one-dimensional (and in the latter case is spanned by a cuspidal
function).

Proof.

Recall that by
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 24.1.6]
, an irreducible local system
\sigma

gives rise to a connected component
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}
isomorphic to

\operatorname{pt}/\operatorname{Aut}(\sigma)
, where
\operatorname{Aut}(\sigma)
is a finite group. In particular
7
7

7

Note also that
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}
is canonically Calabi-Yau,
i.e., the determinant line bundle of its cotangent bundle is trivialized. Hence, the restriction of
\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}}

to the quasi-smooth locus of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}
is canonically isomorphic to the restriction of

{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}}
.
,

\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}}
.

Recall now that the excursion algebra identifies with

H^{0}(\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}})).

The map

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}\to\operatorname{Spec}(\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}})),

induced by

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}\to\operatorname{Spec}(\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}},{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}})),

identifies with

\operatorname{pt}/\operatorname{Aut}(\sigma)\to\operatorname{pt}.

This makes the assertion obvious.

∎

Remark 1.5.9
.

If Conjecture
1.3.10
holds, then
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}
is all of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\check{G}}
.

In particular, in this case in Corollary
1.5.8
, the corresponding eigenspace is
exactly one-dimensional.

1.6.
Proof of Propositions
1.1.5
and
1.2.5

1.6.1.

Write
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}
as a union of its connected components

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}=\underset{\alpha}{\sqcup}\,{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}.

As in
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 21.1]
, we can write each
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}
as a (countable) colimit

{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}\simeq\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,Z_{\alpha,n},

where:

•

Each
Z_{\alpha,n}
is a quasi-smooth algebraic stack;

•

Each
i_{\alpha,n}:Z_{\alpha,n}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}
is a
regular
closed embedding that
induces an isomorphism at the reduced level.

1.6.2.

The category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})
is compactly generated by objects of the form

(i_{\alpha,n})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}})\otimes{\mathcal{E}},

where
{\mathcal{E}}
is a dualizable object in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
.

In fact, we can take
{\mathcal{E}}
to be the pullback of a vector bundle under the evaluation map

\operatorname{ev}_{x}:\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}\to\operatorname{pt}/{\check{G}}

corresponding to some chosen point
x\in X
.

1.6.3.

We now begin the proof of Proposition
1.1.5
.

In the above notations, it suffices to show that the functor

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}
sends each
(i_{\alpha,n})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}})
to
a compact object of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

1.6.4.

Consider the category

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{\alpha,n})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

We have a pair of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear functors

((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id}):{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{\alpha,n})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}):((i_{\alpha,n})^{!}\otimes\operatorname{Id}).

1.6.5.

Let

{\mathsf{P}}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{\alpha,n})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

be as in
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 15.3.2]
. I.e., this is the left adjoint to forgetful functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{\alpha,n})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{emb.Nilp}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

see
[
AGKRRV1
, Corollary 13.5.4]
.

Unwinding the construction (see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 15]
), we obtain that

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}}))\simeq((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathsf{P}}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}}(\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}})).

1.6.6.

Now the object
{\mathsf{P}}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}}(\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}})
is compact in

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{\alpha,n})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

(being the value of a left adjoint on a compact object).

Finally, the functor
(i_{\alpha,n})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id}
preserves compactness since it admits a continuous right
adjoint.

∎[Proposition
1.1.5
]

1.6.7.
Proof of Proposition
1.2.5

Note that

\Gamma_{!}({\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\oplus}\,\Gamma_{!}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha},(-)|_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}}),

while the functor

\Gamma_{!}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha},-):{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{\alpha})\to\operatorname{Vect}

can be written as

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})}((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}}),-).

Thus, we can rewrite the composition

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\Gamma_{!}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

as

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}}),{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}(-)),

and hence by adjunction as

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}\circ(i_{\alpha,n})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}}),-).

By Sect.
1.6.5
, we can rewrite the latter expression as

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}\left(((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathsf{P}}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}}(\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}})),-\right),

and further as

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{\alpha,n})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}\left({\mathsf{P}}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{Z_{\alpha,n}}(\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}}),((i_{\alpha,n})^{!}\otimes\operatorname{Id})(-)\right),

and again by adjunction

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}\left(\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}},\operatorname{emb.Nilp}\circ((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ((i_{\alpha,n})^{!}\otimes\operatorname{Id})(-)\right).

Since
\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}}
is compact, the latter expression identifies with

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}\left(\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}},\operatorname{emb.Nilp}\left(\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ((i_{\alpha,n})^{!}\otimes\operatorname{Id})(-)\right)\right).

The required assertion follows now from the fact that the natural transformation

\underset{\alpha}{\oplus}\,\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,((i_{\alpha,n})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ((i_{\alpha,n})^{!}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\to\operatorname{Id}

on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is an isomorphism.

∎[Proposition
1.2.5
]

1.7.
Proof of Theorem
1.1.10

The proof will largely follow
[
GLC1
, Sect. 2]
.

1.7.1.

For the proof we will assume the validity of Theorem
1.1.7
, which will be proved independently.

Assuming this theorem and using
[
GLC2
, Sect. 2.2]
, we obtain that if
{\mathcal{M}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is compact, then

\operatorname{emb.Nilp}({\mathcal{M}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is bounded below.

Hence, it is enough to show that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}
has a cohomological amplitude bounded
on the left, i.e., there exists an integer
d
such that
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}[-d]
is left t-exact.

1.7.2.

Choose a point
x\in X
, and set

\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr},\operatorname{rigid}_{x}}:=\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\underset{\operatorname{pt}/{\check{G}}}{\times}\operatorname{pt},

where
\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\to\operatorname{pt}/{\check{G}}
is the evaluation map
\operatorname{ev}_{x}
.

According to
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 1.6.3]
, the prestack
\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr},\operatorname{rigid}_{x}}
is a disjoint union
of formal affine schemes. In particular, the functor

\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr},\operatorname{rigid}_{x}},-):{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr},\operatorname{rigid}_{x}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

is t-exact and conservative.

1.7.3.

Note also that we have a canonical isomorphism

\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr},\operatorname{rigid}_{x}},\pi^{*}(-))\simeq\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}},\operatorname{ev}_{x}^{*}(R_{\check{G}})\otimes(-)),

where:

•

\pi
v denotes the projection
\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr},\operatorname{rigid}_{x}}\to\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}
;

•

R_{\check{G}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{pt}/{\check{G}})
is the regular representation.

Hence, we obtain that it is enough to show that the functors

\Gamma_{!}\left(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}},\operatorname{ev}_{x}^{*}(V)\otimes{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}(-)\right),\quad V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\heartsuit}.

have cohomological amplitudes uniformly bounded on the left.

1.7.4.

By Proposition
1.2.5
, we rewrite the above functor as

(1.9)

\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\circ\operatorname{H}_{V,x}(-),

where
\operatorname{H}_{V,x}
is the Hecke endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
corresponding to the chosen
x
and
V
.

We will show that the functors (
1.9
) (for
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})
)
have cohomological amplitudes uniformly bounded on the left on all of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
(and not just
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
).

1.7.5.

Consider the
tempered subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}\overset{{\mathbf{u}}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

as defined in
[
FR
, Sect. 7]
(using the choice of
x\in X
). The embedding
{\mathbf{u}}
admits a continuous right adjoint, denoted
{\mathbf{u}}^{R}
.

The category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}
carries a uniquely defined t-structure for which the functor
{\mathbf{u}}^{R}
is
t-exact, see
[
FR
, Sect. 7.2]
.

Another key feature of this t-structure is that the Hecke functors
\operatorname{H}_{V,x}
descend to
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}

and are t-exact on it (for
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\heartsuit}
), see
[
FR
, Theorem 7.1.0.1]
.

1.7.6.

Note that since
\operatorname{Poinc}_{!}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}
, the functor
\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}
factors
canonically as

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbf{u}}^{R}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}\overset{\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{\operatorname{temp}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

Hence, it is enough to show that the functors

\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{\operatorname{temp}}\circ\operatorname{H}_{V,x}(-):\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}\to\operatorname{Vect}

have cohomological amplitudes uniformly bounded on the left.

1.7.7.

By the t-exactness of the Hecke action on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}
, it suffices to show that the functor

\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{\operatorname{temp}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}\to\operatorname{Vect}

has a cohomological amplitude bounded on the left.

This is in turn equivalent to the functor

\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect}

having a cohomological amplitude bounded on the left, which is obvious.

∎[Theorem
1.1.10
]

2.
Proof of Theorem
1.3.9
in characteristic
0

In this section we will show that when
\operatorname{char}(k)=0
, the functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}}{\to}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})

is an equivalence.

2.1.
Constructible Betti geometric Langlands

In this subsection we take
k
to be the field of complex numbers
{\mathbb{C}}
, and we will work with the sheaf theory
denoted
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}(-)
of (ind-)constructible Betti sheaves with coefficients in an arbitrary field
\mathsf{e}

of characteristic
0
.

2.1.1.

Let
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}}
be the moduli space of local systems with restricted variation,
defined using the constructible sheaf theory
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}(-)
.

The material in Sects.
1.1
-
1.4

applies verbatim and we obtain a functor

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}}).

2.1.2.

We claim:

Theorem 2.1.3
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

The above functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}
is the same as the functor denoted by the same symbol
in
[
GLC1
, Sect. 3.5.3]
.

Now the assertion follows from the validity of the full de Rham version of the geometric Langlands conjecture
(proved in
[
GLC5
]
) combined with
[
GLC1
, Theorem 3.5.6]
.

∎

2.2.
Betti vs étale comparison

In this subsection we continue to assume that
k={\mathbb{C}}
, and we will take
\mathsf{e}:=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
.
We will work with two sheaf theories:

One is
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}(-)=:\operatorname{Shv}(-)
of (ind-)constructible
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-adic étale sheaves,
considered in Sect.
1
.

The other is
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}(-)
considered in Sect.
2.1
above.

We will show that the validity of Theorem
2.1.3
implies the validity of Theorem
1.3.9
(ii)
(for
k={\mathbb{C}}
).

2.2.1.

Note that we have a fully faithful natural transformation between the sheaf theories

(2.1)

(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti}):\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}(-)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}(-)

that commutes with both !- and *- direct and inverse images, see

[
SGA4(3)
, Theorems XI.4.4, XVI.4.1 and XVII.5.3.3]
.

At the level of compact objets (i.e., constructible sheaves) on affine schemes, its essential image
is characterized as follows:

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}(Y)^{c}
, and let
Y_{\alpha}
be a decomposition of
Y

into smooth locally closed subsets, such that the restrictions (either *- or !-) of
{\mathcal{F}}
to the strata are
lisse. Consider the individual cohomology sheaves
H^{i}({\mathcal{F}}|_{Y_{\alpha}})
as representations
V_{\alpha,i}

of
\pi_{1}(Y_{\alpha})
(for some chosen base point).

Then
{\mathcal{F}}
lies in the essential image of
(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti}))_{Y}
if and only if each
V_{\alpha,i}

admits a
\pi_{1}(Y_{\alpha})
-invariant lattice with respect to
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}\subset E\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
for
a finite extension
E\supseteq{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.

2.2.2.

In particular, the natural transformation (
2.1
) induces a fully
faithful functor

(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti})_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

which restricts to a (fully faithful) functor

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

2.2.3.

Recall that Theorem
1.1.7
has been proved for

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

in

[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 16.4.10]
. Hence, it holds for

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}^{\operatorname{et}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

as well.

2.2.4.

Let

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}}\text{ and }\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}}

be the two versions of the be the moduli space of local systems with restricted variation.

The functor

(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti})_{X}:\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{et}}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}(X)^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}

is symmetric monoidal, and hence induces a map between the corresponding moduli spaces

(2.2)

(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti})_{\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}}:\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}}\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}}.

We claim:

Lemma 2.2.5
.

The map (
2.2
) factors through an isomorphism from
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}}

to the disjoint union of some of the connected components of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}}
.

Proof.

Follows from the description of essential image of the natural transformation
(
2.1
) in Sect.
2.2.1
, see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 9.5.8]
.

∎

Remark 2.2.6
.

An assertion parallel to Lemma
2.2.5
holds for any full symmetric monoidal
subcategory of
\operatorname{Lisse}(X)^{\heartsuit}
.

2.2.7.

Thus, we can regard

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

as a full subcategory (in fact, a direct summand) of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, and it is easy to see
that the functor

(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti})_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

lands in it: this follows from the fact that this functor is compatible with the actions of

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})
, viewed as a direct factor of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}}
on the two sides.

Similarly, direct image along
(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti})_{\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}}
is an equivalence

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\sim}{\to}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}).

Furthermore, it follows from Proposition
1.2.5
that we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti})_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\\
@V{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}}V{}V@V{\sim}V{\operatorname{Id}\otimes{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}}V\\
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})@>{(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti})_{\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}}}>{\sim}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}}),\end{CD}

in which the top horizontal arrow is fully faithful.

Hence, we obtain that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}
is fully faithful. Thus, in order to prove that it is an equivalence,
it remains to show that the essential image of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}
generates the target.

2.2.8.

Write

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}=({\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}})_{\operatorname{red}}\sqcup({\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}})_{\operatorname{irred}};

these are unions of connected components that correspond to reducible (resp., irreducible) local systems.

It suffices to show that the essential image of each of the corresponding functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(({\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}})_{\operatorname{red}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(({\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}})_{\operatorname{red}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\simeq{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}((\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}})

and

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(({\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}})_{\operatorname{irred}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(({\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}})_{\operatorname{irred}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\simeq{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}((\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{irrred}})

generates its target category.

This is in turn equivalent to showing that the essential image of the each of the functors

(2.3)

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\operatorname{restriction}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}((\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}})

and

(2.4)

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\operatorname{restriction}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}((\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{irred}})

generates the target category.

2.2.9.

We first prove the assertion for (
2.3
). By induction on the semi-simple rank, we may
assume that Theorem
1.3.9
(ii) holds for proper Levi subgroups of
G
.

Note that
8
8

8

In
loc. cit.
this is proved in the de Rham context, but the argument applies in any sheaf-theoretic context.

by
[
AG1
, Theorem 13.3.6]
, the union of the essential images of the functors

{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{M}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})

for proper Levi subgroups generates
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}((\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}})
,
viewed as a full subcategory in
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})
.

Hence, the required generation assertion follows from Theorem
1.4.6
.

2.2.10.

We now show that (
2.4
) generates the target category. Note that the functor

{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec}}
(see Sect.
1.3.1
) induces an
equivalence

{\operatorname{QCoh}}((\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{irred}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}((\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{irred}}).

Hence, it is enough to show that the essential image of the functor

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\operatorname{restriction}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}((\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{irred}})

generates the target category.

We will show that the essential image of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}
itself generates the target category.
First, we note that this functor is fully faithful (indeed, our situation embeds fully faithfully into the Betti situation, where the functor
in question is an equivalence). Hence, it is enough to show that its
left
9
9

9

Note that without fully faithfulness,
generation of the target is equivalent to the fact that the
right
adjoint be consevative.
adjoint,
i.e., the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}^{\operatorname{et,restr},L}
,
is conservative.

Note, however, that the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{et}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@<{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}^{\operatorname{et,restr},L}}<{}<{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti})_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}V{}V@V{}V{(\operatorname{et}\to\operatorname{Betti})_{\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Betti,constr}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@<{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{temp}}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr},L}}<{\sim}<{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}})\end{CD}

is commutative: indeed, the two circuits are compatible with the spectral action
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}})

(which we think as a direct factor of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{Betti,restr}}_{\check{G}})
) and send
{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{et,restr}}_{\check{G}}}

to (the Betti version of)
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
.

Since the right vertical arrow in the diagram is conservative, and the bottom horizontal arrow
is an equivalence, we obtain that the top horizontal arrow is conservative, as required.

∎[Theorem
1.3.9
(ii) for
k={\mathbb{C}}
]

2.3.
Proof of Theorem
1.3.9
(ii) for an arbitrary
k
of char.
0

The proof will use the Lefschetz principle.

2.3.1.

Note that if
k_{1}\subset k_{2}
is an extension of algebraically closed fields of characteristic
0
,
for a prestack
{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}
over
k_{1}
and its base change
{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}
to
k_{2}
, the pullback functor

(2.5)

\operatorname{Lisse}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Lisse}({\mathcal{Z}}_{2})

is an equivalence, see
[
SGA1
, Proposition XIII.4.3]
.

This formally implies that the pullback functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}_{2})

is fully faithful.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})
be a conical Lagrangian subset. It follows from (
2.5
)
that the pullback functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{1}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{2}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{2})

is an equivalence.

2.3.2.

In particular, for a curve
X_{1}
defined over
k_{1}
, and its base change
X_{2}
to
k_{2}
, the map

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}(X_{1})\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}(X_{2})

is an isomorphism (as prestacks over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
).

2.3.3.

Given the curve
X
over
k
, let
k^{\prime}
be a countably generated field over which it is defined;
denote the resulting curve over
k^{\prime}
by
X^{\prime}
.

We obtain a commutative diagram
