\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}(X^{\prime}))@>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}(X^{\prime})}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\prime}))\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}(X))@>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G}(X)}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}(X)).\end{CD}

Hence, the validity of Theorem
1.3.9
(ii) over
k
is equivalent to its validity over
k^{\prime}
.

2.3.4.

Embedding
k^{\prime}
into
{\mathbb{C}}
, we obtain that the validity of Theorem
1.3.9
(ii) over
k^{\prime}
is equivalent to its validity over
{\mathbb{C}}
.
However, the latter has been established in Sect.
2.2
above.

∎[Theorem
1.3.9
(ii)]

3.
The specialization functor

In this section we introduce a procedure that will allow us to deduce information about the
Langlands functor in characteristic
p
from its counterpart in characteristic
0
.

This procedure essentially amounts to taking nearby cycles for a family of curves over a DVR.

3.1.
Axiomatics for the functor

3.1.1.

Let
{\mathsf{k}}
be an (algebraically closed) field of positive characteristic. Let
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}:=\operatorname{Witt}({\mathsf{k}})

be the ring of Witt vectors of
{\mathsf{k}}
, let
{\mathsf{K}}_{0}
denote the field of fractions of
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}
and let

{\mathsf{K}}
denote the algebraic closure of
{\mathsf{K}}_{0}
. Let
{\mathsf{R}}
denote the integral closure of
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}

in
{\mathsf{K}}
.

Given a (smooth complete) curve
X_{\mathsf{k}}
over
{\mathsf{k}}
, we can
choose
its extension to a (smooth complete) curve

X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
. Let
X_{\mathsf{K}}
be the base change of
X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
to
K
.

3.1.2.
Notational convention

We will insert subscripts
{\mathsf{k}}
,
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}
or
{\mathsf{K}}
into the corresponding geometric objects in order to
specify which situation we are working with.

A symbol without such a subscript (e.g., just
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
) means that the discussion applies
to any of the above situations.

3.1.3.

Note that restriction along
X_{\mathsf{k}}\to X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
defines an equivalence

\operatorname{QLisse}(X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})\simeq\operatorname{QLisse}(X_{\mathsf{k}}).

Restriction along
X_{\mathsf{K}}\to X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
defines a
fully faithful
functor

\operatorname{QLisse}(X_{\mathsf{R}})\to\operatorname{QLisse}(X_{\mathsf{K}}).

From here we obtain an embedding

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}\overset{\iota}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{K}}},

which identifies
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}
with the union of
some of
the
connected components of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{K}}}
.

3.1.4.

Consider the corresponding moduli stacks

\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}.

Consider the full subcategory (in fact, a direct summand)
10
10

10

We emphasize that there is no such object as
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})
;
only
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})
.

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}):={\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{K}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}).

3.1.5.

We will prove:

Theorem 3.1.6
.

There exists a functor

(3.1)

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})

with the following properties:

•

(A) The functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
is a Verdier quotient.

•

(B) The functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
intertwines the actions of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})
.

•

(C) The functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
sends
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
to
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{k}}}
,
where
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
is the direct summand of
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{K}}}
that belongs to

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})
.

•

(D) The functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
makes the diagrams

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})@>{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}},M}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{k}}})\\
@V{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!,{\mathsf{K}}}}V{}V@V{}V{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!,{\mathsf{k}}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})@>{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}},G}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\end{CD}

commute.

•

(E) The functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
is t-exact.

This theorem will be proved in the course of Sects.
5
-
8
. For the rest of this section
we will assume Theorem
3.1.6
. We will derive some further properties of the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}

of (
3.1
), as well as consequences for the category

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
that follow from the above properties.

3.1.7.

We claim that the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
of (
3.1
) preserves compactness.

Indeed, the validity of Theorem
1.3.9
for
{\mathsf{K}}
implies that the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})

has compact generators of the form:

(i)
{\mathcal{E}}\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
, where
{\mathcal{E}}
is a dualizable object in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})
;

(ii)
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{M})
for
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})^{c}
.

Now, objects of the form

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{E}}\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})

are compact in
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
by Properties (B) and (C), and objects of the form

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{M}))

are compact in
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
by Property (D) and induction on semi-simple rank.

3.1.8.

Note that combining with Property (A), we obtain:

Corollary 3.1.9
.

Objects of the form

(i)
{\mathcal{E}}\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{k}}}
,
{\mathcal{E}}
is a dualizable object in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})
;

(ii)
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{M})
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{k}}})^{c}

compactly generate
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
.

Remark 3.1.10
.

Note that Corollary
3.1.9
is as an extension to positive characteristic
of the main result of
[
FR
]
.

One can similarly show that objects of type (i) in Corollary
3.1.9

generate the
tempered
subcategory of
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{k}}})
.

Remark 3.1.11
.

We conjecture that the entire
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
is generated by objects of the form:

(i)
{\mathcal{F}}\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{k}}}
,
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
,

(ii)
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{M})
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{k}}})^{c}
.

We can prove this over a ground field of characteristic
0
, but so far not over a field
of positive characteristic.

3.1.12.
Proof of Theorem
1.1.7

We can now deduce Theorem
1.1.7
for
{\mathsf{k}}
.

We need to show that compact generators of
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
are compact as objects of

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
.

By Corollary
3.1.9
, it suffices to show to show that the objects

{\mathcal{E}}\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{k}}}\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{E}}\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})

and

{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{M}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{M}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{k}}})^{c}

are compact in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
.

For objects of the second type, this is clear by induction on the semi-simple rank, since the functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}

preserves compactness. Thus, it remains to deal with objects of the first type.

According to
[
AGKRRV1
, Proposition 16.4.7]
, the validity of Theorem
1.1.7
(in a given context)
is equivalent to the fact that the compact generators of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
are eventually coconnective.

Hence, this property holds for
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})
by Sect.
2.2.3
. In particular,
the objects
{\mathcal{E}}\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
are eventually coconnective. Now Property (E)
implies that the objects

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{E}}\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathsf{K}}})

are also eventually coconnective.

∎[Theorem
1.1.7
]

3.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.3.9
(i)

In this subsection we will show that the existence of the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
with Properties (A)-(E)
specified in Theorem
3.1.6
allows us to deduce Theorem
1.3.9
(i) from Theorem
1.3.9
(ii).

3.2.1.

First, note that the validity of Theorem
1.3.9
(ii) for
{\mathsf{K}}
implies that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}
induces an equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\overset{\sim}{\to}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{K}}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{K}}})\simeq{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}});

denote it by
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
.

3.2.2.

Set

(3.2)

^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}:=\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ({\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})^{-1},\quad{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}).

Note that it follows from Property (C) that

(3.3)

^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\circ{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec}}\simeq{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp},k}.

3.2.3.

We will prove:

Proposition 3.2.4
.

The functor
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
is the left adjoint of
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
.

The proof will be given in Sect.
3.3
below. We proceed with the proof of Theorem
1.3.9
(i).
Assuming the proposition, we will denote

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}:={}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}.

3.2.5.

As a formal corollary of Proposition
3.2.4
combined with Property (A), we obtain:

Corollary 3.2.6
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
is fully faithful.

Thus, to prove Theorem
1.3.9
(ii) it remains to show that the essential image of
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}

equals

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}),

where
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}
is the disjoint union of some of the connected components
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}
.

3.2.7.

Since the functors involved are
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})
-linear, we can work with
one connected component of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}
at a time. I.e., we need to show that for
a given connected component
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}
, either the adjoint functors

(3.4)

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\alpha}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\leftrightarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}):{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,k,\alpha}

are mutually inverse equivalences, or the left-hand side is zero.

We consider separately
the cases when a given connected component corresponds to reducible or irreducible
local systems.

3.2.8.

We start with the irreducible case. Note that the inclusion

{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})

is an equality.

Hence, the composition

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\alpha}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,k,\alpha},

being a
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})
-linear endofunctor of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})
,
is given by tensoring by a unital algebra object
11
11

11

The algebra structure comes from the fact
that
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\alpha}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,k,\alpha}
is a
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})
-linear monad.

{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}).

We need to show that either the unit map

(3.5)

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}

is an isomorphism, or
{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}=0
.

By Barr-Beck-Lurie, the right adjoint in (
3.4
) identifies the left-hand side
with

{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})).

By Corollary
3.2.6
, the forgetful functor

(3.6)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}))\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})

is fully faithful.

Let

i_{\sigma}:\operatorname{pt}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}

correspond to the (unique) closed point of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}
.

Applying to (
3.6
) the operation
\operatorname{Vect}\underset{i_{\sigma}^{*},{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})}{\otimes}(-)
,
we obtain a fully faithful functor

i_{\sigma}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}){\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{Vect})\to\operatorname{Vect}.

This easily implies that either
\mathsf{e}\to i_{\sigma}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha})
is an isomorphism, or
i_{\sigma}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha})=0
.
The latter dichotomy implies that one of the above two possibilities for
{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}
itself must hold.

3.2.9.

We now consider the
reducible
case. Let
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma}
be a connected
component whose closed point
\sigma
is a semi-simple local system. Then there
exists a unique conjugacy class of Levi subgroups
M
(see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sects. 3.6 and 3.7]
) such that

\sigma
factors via an
irreducible

{\check{M}}
-local system
\sigma_{\check{M}}
.

By induction on the semi-simple rank, we can assume that the assertion of Theorem
1.3.9
(ii)
is valid for
M
. Let
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma_{\check{M}}}
be the corresponding connected
component of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}}}
.

We consider the following two cases of the behavior of the functor (
3.4
)
with
G
replaced by
M
and
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}:=\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma_{\check{M}}}
:

(a) It is an equivalence;

(b) The left-hand side is zero.

3.2.10.

We claim that in case (a), the functor (
3.4
) for
G
and
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}:=\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma}

is an equivalence.

Indeed, it is enough to show that the essential image of
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\alpha}
generates the target category.
Note that
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})
is generated by the essential
images of the functors

{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})

for the parabolics in the given class of association.

Now the required assertion follows from Theorem
1.4.6
(for
{\mathsf{k}}
).

3.2.11.

We claim that in case (b), the left adjoint in (
3.4
) for
G
and
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}:=\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma}
,
is zero. This will show that the left-hand side is zero (by Corollary
3.2.6
).

By construction and Property (B), we can identify the functor in question with

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}).

We will show that the above functor annihilates the generators. By Property (D), we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma_{\check{M}}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})@>{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}V\\
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma_{\check{M}}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{k}}})@>{}>{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}).\end{CD}

We claim that the top horizontal arrows in these diagrams, taken for all the parabolics in the given class of association,
generate the target. This follows from the combination of:

(i) The fact that
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}
is an equivalence;

(ii) Theorem
1.4.6
for
{\mathsf{K}}
;

(iii) The corresponding fact on the spectral side.

Hence, it suffices to show that the anti-clockwise circuits in these diagrams vanish. However, this follows
from the fact that the lower-left corner vanishes (this is the assumption in case (b)).

∎[Theorem
1.3.9
(i)]

3.2.12.
Proof of Theorem
1.3.9
(iii)

We need to show that in the notations of (
3.4
), the category

(3.7)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{GL_{n}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{GL_{n}})

is non-zero for every connected component
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{GL_{n}}
.

By induction, we may assume that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{GL_{n^{\prime}}}
is an equivalence for
n^{\prime}<n
.
In particular, it is an equivalence for proper Levi subgroups of
G=GL_{n}
. Hence, in the notations of
Sect.
3.2.9
only scenario (a) occurs. In particular, the category (
3.7
) is
non-zero whenever
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}
corresponds to reducible local systems.

It remains to treat the case of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}
corresponding to an irreducible local system
\sigma
.
It is enough to show that
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{GL_{n}})
contains a non-zero Hecke eigensheaf corresponding
to
\sigma
. However, this has been established in
[
FGV
,
Ga1
]
.

∎[Theorem
1.3.9
(iii)]

3.3.
Proof of Proposition
3.2.4

3.3.1.

Note that the functor
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
preserves compactness (see Sect.
3.1.7
).
Hence, it is enough to show that for

{\mathcal{F}}\in{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})^{c}\text{ and }{\mathcal{M}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})^{c},

there is a canonical isomorphism

(3.8)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})}({}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathcal{M}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{M}})).

3.3.2.

Consider the tempered quotients

{\mathbf{u}}^{R}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}:{\mathbf{u}}

and

{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})\leftrightarrows{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}):{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec}}.

By construction, the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
induces a functor

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})

such that

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{temp},k}\circ{\mathbf{u}}^{R}\simeq{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse},{\mathsf{k}}}\simeq{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}.

In particular, the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}
takes values in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}

(viewed as a subcategory) and provides a left adjoint to
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}
.

3.3.3.

By Proposition
4.5.2
below, the functor
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
induces a functor

{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}},

so that

{\mathbf{u}}^{R}\circ{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\simeq{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}\circ{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}.

From (
3.3
) we obtain that

{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}\simeq{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}.

In particular, we obtain that the functors

(3.9)

{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}\leftrightarrows{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}):{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}

do form an adjoint pair.

3.3.4.

Consider the maps

(3.10)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})}({}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathcal{M}})\to{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}}({\mathbf{u}}^{R}\circ{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathbf{u}}^{R}({\mathcal{M}}))=\\
={\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}}({}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,\operatorname{temp},k}\circ{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathbf{u}}^{R}({\mathcal{M}}))\overset{\text{adjunction\,\eqref{e:adj 2}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}({\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{temp},{\mathsf{k}}}\circ{\mathbf{u}}^{R}({\mathcal{M}}))=\\
={\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}({\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{M}}))\leftarrow{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{M}}))

In order to establish (
3.8
), it suffices to show that the first and the last arrow in (
3.10
) are isomorphisms.

For the last arrow, this follows from the fact that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
sends compact objects to eventually
coconnective objects (by construction), and the functor
{\mathbf{u}}^{\operatorname{spec},R}

is fully faithful on the eventually coconnective subcategory.

For the first arrow, since
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})
is compact (see Sect.
3.1.7
), the assertion
follows from the next lemma:

Lemma 3.3.5
.

The restriction of the functor
{\mathbf{u}}^{R}
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})^{c}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

Repeats verbatim the proof of
[
GLC1
, Proposition 5.2.3]
.

∎

∎[Proposition
3.2.4
]

3.4.
An alternative proof of Theorem
1.3.9
(i) and Proposition
3.2.4

This proof will use an additional property of the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
, given by Remark

7.4.3
.

3.4.1.

Let

{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})

be defined as in (
3.2
).

We already know that this functor preserves compactness and by Property (A) of the functor (
3.1
),
it is a Verdier quotient. I.e., the functor
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
admits a
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})
-linear
fully faithful right adjoint. Denote it by
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
(note, however, that we do know yet that

{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
).

We wish to show that
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
is an equivalence onto a direct summand of
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\mathsf{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})

corresponding to the union of some of the connected
components of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}
.

3.4.2.

Inspecting the argument in Sects.
3.2.7
-
3.2.11
, we see that the only place where we
used that
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}={\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
was in case (a) in Sect.
3.2.10
. We provide an alternative argument as follows:

It suffices to prove that the functor
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr},L}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}|_{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})}

is conservative. In other words, we wish to show that in case (a) the functor

(3.11)

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})

is conservative.

According to Remark
7.4.3
, we have commutative diagrams

(3.12)

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})@>{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{M}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{k}}})\\
@A{\operatorname{CT}^{-}_{*}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{CT}^{-}_{*}}A\\
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})@>{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}},\sigma})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}),\end{CD}

where
\operatorname{CT}^{-}_{*}
denotes the constant term functor.

Now, the validity of GLC over
{\mathsf{K}}
implies that the direct sums of the functors
\operatorname{CT}^{-}_{*}
over the
parabolics in the given class of association (i.e., the left vertical arrow in (
3.12
))
is conservative.

By induction on the semi-simple rank we can assume that the top horizontal arrow in (
3.12
)
is conservative.

Hence, the bottom the top horizontal arrow in (
3.12
)
is also conservative, as desired.

∎[Theorem
1.3.9
(i)]

3.4.3.

We now give an alternative argument for Proposition
3.2.4
. Namely, we wish to show that

{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}={\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
. We will show that
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}

satisfies the two conditions of Corollary
1.3.6
.

We already know that
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
induces an equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({}^{\prime}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{k}}).

In particular, it sends compact objects to compact (and, in particular, eventually
coconnective) ones. Hence,
{}^{\prime}{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}
satisfies condition (i)
of Corollary
1.3.6
.

Condition (ii) follows by passing to right adjoints from (
3.3
).

∎[Proposition
3.2.4
]

4.
Proof of Theorem
3.1.6

In this section we begin the proof of Theorem
3.1.6
. Namely, we will
construct the functor
\operatorname{Sp}
and establish Properties (B)-(E).

Property (A) will be dealt with in the next section.

4.1.
Construction of the specialization functor

4.1.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
be a scheme or algebraic stack over
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}
. Our goal is
to define a functor

(4.1)

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}).

Denote

\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{k}})\overset{{\mathbf{i}}}{\to}\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}})\overset{{\mathbf{j}}}{\leftarrow}\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{K}}).

We will use the same symbols
{\mathbf{i}}
and
{\mathbf{j}}
for the corresponding maps

{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}}\leftarrow{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}.

Euphemistically, the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
is given by

(4.2)

{\mathbf{i}}^{*}\circ{\mathbf{j}}_{*}.

Note, however, that
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}})
is non-Noetherian. In what follows we will rewrite the definition of

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
in terms of functors that only use algebro-geometric
objects of finite type.

4.1.2.

First, covering
{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
smoothly be (affine) schemes, it is sufficient to define
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}

for
{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}=S_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
, where
S_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
is an affine scheme of finite type over
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}
,
provided that it commutes with pullbacks along smooth maps
S^{\prime}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\to S_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
.

Second, since
\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{K}})
is by definition the ind-completion of
\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{K}})^{c}
, it suffices to define

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
as a functor
\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{K}})^{c}\to\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{k}})^{c}
.

Third, by the definition of
\operatorname{Shv}(-)^{c}
, this category is the colimit of
\operatorname{Shv}(-)_{E}^{c}
, over
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\subset E\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
,
where
E
is a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
. Hence, it suffices to define

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
as a functor
\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{K}})_{E}^{c}\to\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{k}})_{E}^{c}
.

Fourth, by the definition of
\operatorname{Shv}(-)_{E}^{c}
, it is obtained as the localization with respect to
\ell
of
\operatorname{Shv}(-)^{c}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}
.
Hence, it suffices to define

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
as a functor
\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{K}})_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}^{c}\to\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{k}})_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}^{c}
.

Finally, by the definition of
\operatorname{Shv}(-)_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}^{c}
, it is obtained as

\underset{n}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(-)_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/\ell^{n}}^{c},

where the limit is taken in the
\infty
-category of
non-cocomplete
DG categorries.

Hence, it suffices to define
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
as a functor

(4.3)

\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{K}})_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/\ell^{n}}^{c}\to\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{k}})_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/\ell^{n}}^{c}.

4.1.3.

Note that

\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{K}})_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/\ell^{n}}^{c}=\underset{{\mathsf{K}}^{\prime}_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(S_{{\mathsf{K}}^{\prime}_{0}})_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/\ell^{n}}^{c},

where:

•

The colimit is taken in the
\infty
-category of
non-cocomplete
DG categorries;

•

The index category is the poset of finite extensions
{\mathsf{K}}^{\prime}_{0}\supset{\mathsf{K}}_{0}
.

We proceed to define the corresponding functors

(4.4)

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}^{\prime}_{0}\to{\mathsf{k}}}:\operatorname{Shv}(S_{{\mathsf{K}}^{\prime}_{0}})_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/\ell^{n}}^{c}\to\operatorname{Shv}(S_{\mathsf{k}})_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/\ell^{n}}^{c}.

4.1.4.

Let
{\mathsf{R}}^{\prime}_{0}
denote the integral closure of
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}
in
{\mathsf{K}}^{\prime}_{0}
. Consider the diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}@>{}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}^{\prime}_{0}}@<{}<{}<{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{K}}^{\prime}_{0}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{k}})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}^{\prime}_{0})@<{}<{}<\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{K}}^{\prime}_{0}).\end{CD}

We define (
4.4
) to be the nearby cycles functor
\Psi
.

4.1.5.

Note that, by construction, the composition

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{K}}_{0}})\overset{\text{pullback}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}})\overset{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}})

is the usual nearby cycles functor

\Psi:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{K}}_{0}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}).

4.1.6.

We record the following properties of the functor (
4.1
):

(1)

It is t-exact;

(2)

It sends constructible
12
12

12

Constructible:=pullback to an affine scheme by means of a
smooth morphism is constructible (i.e., compact). Note, however, that for stacks “constructible”
does not imply “compact”: the obstruction is (i) non-quasi-compactness of the stack and
(ii) non-trivial stabilizers.
objects in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}})
to constructible objects in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}})
;

(3)

For
{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}={\mathcal{Y}}^{1}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}^{2}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}^{i}_{\mathsf{K}})
, we have

(4.5)

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\boxtimes\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

(4)

For a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}^{1}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}^{2}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
we have the natural transformations

(4.6)

(f_{\mathsf{k}})_{!}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(f_{\mathsf{K}})_{!},\,\,\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(f_{\mathsf{K}})_{*}\to(f_{\mathsf{k}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}},

and

(4.7)

(f_{\mathsf{k}})^{*}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(f_{\mathsf{K}})^{*},\,\,\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(f_{\mathsf{K}})^{!}\to(f_{\mathsf{k}})^{!}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}},

where
f_{\mathsf{k}}
(resp.,
f_{\mathsf{K}}
) denotes the fiber of
f
over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{k}})
(resp.,
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{K}})
).

(5)

The maps (
4.6
) are mutually inverse isomorphisms when
f
is proper and
the maps (
4.7
) are mutually inverse isomorphisms (up to a shift by the relative dimension) when
f
is smooth.

(6)

It commutes with Verdier duality on constructible objects

(4.8)

{\mathbb{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}}(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathbb{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}}({\mathcal{F}}));

moreover, for an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
,
the following diagram commutes

(4.9)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}}(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}))@>{\sim}>{\operatorname{id}\boxtimes\text{\eqref{e:Sp and Verdier 0}}}>\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathbb{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}}({\mathcal{F}}))\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}}),\end{CD}

where:

•

The symbol
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
in the bottom-right corner refers to the functor (
4.1
)
for
{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
;

•

The bottom horizontal arrow comes from the identification
\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{k}})}\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{K}})})

and the natural transformation

(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}})_{!}\circ\pi_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}}^{*}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}})_{!}\circ\pi_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}}^{*}

where
\pi_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{-}}:{\mathcal{Y}}_{-}\to\operatorname{Spec}(-)
is the relevant structural map.

Remark 4.1.7
.

Suppose
\alpha:{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{G}}

is a morphism of constructible sheaves. From (
4.9
)
we obtain a tautological commutative diagram

(4.10)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{G}})\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}}(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}))@>{\sim}>{\operatorname{id}\boxtimes\text{\eqref{e:Sp and Verdier 0}}}>\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{G}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathbb{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}}({\mathcal{F}}))\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}}).\end{CD}

In this formulation, there is some additional homotopy coherence to note.
Each term of (
4.10
)
can now be considered as a functor
from
\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}))^{\operatorname{constr}}
, the
twisted arrow category of constructible sheaves,
to
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}\times_{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{k}})}{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}})
. Then the
more functorial assertion of (
6
) is that there is an commutative square of
functors (
4.10
).

4.1.8.

The following observation will be used in the sequel:

Lemma 4.1.9
.

Let
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
be a map of algebraic stacks over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.
Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})
be ULA over
{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
. Then for any

{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathsf{K}}})
, the naturally defined map

{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathsf{k}}}\overset{*}{\otimes}(f_{{\mathsf{k}}}^{*}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{Z}}))\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathsf{K}}}\overset{*}{\otimes}f_{{\mathsf{K}}}^{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}))

is a isomorphism, where
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathsf{k}}}
(resp.,
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathsf{K}}}
) denotes the *-restriction of
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
to

{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{k}}
(resp.,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}}
)

Remark 4.1.10
.

Note that when
{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
, the ULA condition simply means that

\Phi({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})=0,

where
\Phi
is the vanishing cycles functor.

In other words, this is equivalent to the map

{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathsf{K}}})

being an isomorphism.

4.1.11.

In what follows we will study the resulting functor

(4.11)

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}).

4.2.
Specialization and Hecke functors

4.2.1.

Consider the version of the Hecke stack over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
:

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}@<{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}<{}<\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}@>{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}}>{}>\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\\
&&@V{}V{s}V\\
&&X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}.\end{CD}

For an irreducible representation

V^{\lambda}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}}),\quad\lambda\in\Lambda^{+},

let

\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}(V^{\lambda})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

be the object
13
13

13

The cohomological shift
[-2]
in the formula below is designed in order to offset

\dim(X)+\dim({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

\operatorname{IC}_{\overline{\operatorname{Hecke}}^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}[-2].

We claim:

Theorem 4.2.2
.

The object
\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}(V^{\lambda})
restricts to

\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X,{\mathsf{K}}}(V^{\lambda})
and
\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X,{\mathsf{k}}}(V^{\lambda})
, respectively, and is ULA over

\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
with respect to either of the projections.

We will prove this theorem in Sect.
6.1
.

4.2.3.

For a finite set
{\mathcal{I}}
, consider the corresponding
{\mathcal{I}}
-legged Hecke stack:

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}@<{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}<{}<\operatorname{Hecke}_{X^{\mathcal{I}}}@>{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}}>{}>\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\\
&&@V{}V{s}V\\
&&X^{\mathcal{I}}.\end{CD}

As in the usual geometric Satake theory, Theorem
4.2.2
allows us to construct a family of functors

(4.12)

\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}:{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes{\mathcal{I}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}).

Moreover, the essential image of the functor (
4.12
) lies in the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

that consists of objects that are ULA over
\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
(in fact, over
\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X^{\mathcal{I}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
).

4.2.4.

Consider the Hecke functor

\operatorname{H}_{V}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{{\mathcal{I}}}),\quad(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s)_{!}\left(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{*}(-)\overset{*}{\otimes}\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}}}(V)\right),\quad V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes{\mathcal{I}}}.

The functors (
4.12
) give rise to natural transformations

(4.13)

\operatorname{H}_{V,{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\operatorname{H}_{V,{\mathsf{K}}}

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\times X^{{\mathcal{I}}}_{{\mathsf{k}}}).

We claim:

Proposition 4.2.5
.

The natural transformations (
4.13
) are isomorphisms.

Proof.

The map

\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s:\operatorname{Hecke}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{\mathcal{I}}

is proper, hence

(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s)_{!}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s)_{!}

is an isomorphism.

Now, the natural transformation

(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{*}(-)\overset{*}{\otimes}\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}}}(V))\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{*}(-)\overset{*}{\otimes}\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}}}(V))

is also an isomorphism by Lemma
4.1.9
and the ULA property of the objects

\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}(V)
.

∎

4.2.6.

The functors
\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
,
{\mathcal{I}}\in\operatorname{fSet}
make the following system of diagrams of functors
commute:

(4.14)

\begin{CD}{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}@>{\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}_{1}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}_{1}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})\\
@V{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\phi}}V{}V@V{}V{\Delta^{*}_{\phi}}V\\
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes{\mathcal{I}}_{2}}@>{\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}_{2}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}_{X^{{\mathcal{I}}_{2}},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}),\end{CD}

for
\phi:{\mathcal{I}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{I}}_{2}
, where:

•

The left vertical arrow is induced by the symmetric monoidal structure on
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})
;

•

The map
\Delta_{\phi}
is (the base change of) the diagonal map
X^{{\mathcal{I}}_{2}}\to X^{{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}
.

This leads to a system of commutative diagrams of functors

\begin{CD}(\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\times\Delta_{\phi})^{*}\circ\operatorname{H}_{V,{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}@>{}>{}>(\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\times\Delta_{\phi})^{*}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\operatorname{H}_{V,{\mathsf{K}}}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{H}_{\operatorname{Res}_{\phi}(V),{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\operatorname{H}_{\operatorname{Res}_{\phi}(V),{\mathsf{K}}},\end{CD}

which also depends functorially on
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes{\mathcal{I}}}
.

4.3.
Compatibility with Beilinson’s projector

4.3.1.

Recall Beilinson’s projector, denoted
{\mathsf{P}}
, which is an idempotent acting on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

with essential image
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 13.4]
.

We will denote by
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathsf{k}}
(resp.,
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathsf{K}}
) the corresponding endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})

(resp.,
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
).

Let now
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
be a version of
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathsf{K}}
, where in we take

\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\mathsf{G}},{\mathsf{k}}},-)
instead of
\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\mathsf{G}},{\mathsf{K}}},-)
.
This is an idempotent endomorphism of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})
, equal to the composition of

{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathsf{K}}
and the projection onto the direct summand

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}).

4.3.2.

We claim:

Proposition 4.3.3
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}\simeq{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathsf{k}}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}.

Proof.

By construction (see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 13.1.11]
), the functor
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
is the colimit of functors
{\mathsf{F}}_{I}
,
I\in\operatorname{fSet}
,
each of which is the composition of functors of the following form:

•

(a)
\operatorname{H}_{V_{I}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})
for
V_{I}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}
;

•

(b)
(-)\otimes\operatorname{Ev}(V^{\prime}_{I}):\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
,
where:

–

V^{\prime}_{I}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}
;

–

\operatorname{Ev}(V^{\prime}_{I})\in\operatorname{QLisse}(X^{I})\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
is the tautological object corresponding to
V^{\prime}_{I}
.

•

(c)
\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}},-)
.

The functors in (a) commute with
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
by Proposition
4.2.5
. The functors in (b)
commute with
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
by Lemma
4.1.9
. The functors in (c) commute with

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
tautologically.

∎

Remark 4.3.4
.

For future use we remark that both Proposition
4.2.5

4.3.3

remain valid if instead of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
we consider

\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}},

for some stack
{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
. The proofs remain the same.

4.3.5.

From Proposition
4.3.3
we obtain:

Corollary 4.3.6
.

The functor (
4.11
) sends

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})

to

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}).

Remark 4.3.7
.

Note that Proposition
4.3.3
implies that the functor (
4.11
) sends all of

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
, while killing the direct summands
of
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})
that are supported off

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{k}}}\subset\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{G}},{\mathsf{K}}}.

This follows from the fact that the functor
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
applied to
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})

acts as a projector on
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})
.

Remark 4.3.8
.

One can also prove that the functor (
4.11
) sends
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})

as follows:

It follows from
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 14.4.3]
that the subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

can be characterized as follows: it consists of those objects
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
for which for every
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})

the object

\operatorname{H}_{V}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X)

belongs to the full subcategory
14
14

14

In
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 14.4.3]
, this is formulated as belonging to

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{qLisse}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X)
; however, the apparently weaker condition of belonging to

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)
is equivalent to this stronger condition: objects in the essential image of
\operatorname{H}_{V}
are ULA
over
X
, and any object in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)
that is ULA over
X
lies in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{qLisse}(X)
.

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X).

Now, this property is preserved by the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
by (
4.5
).

4.3.9.

Thus, thanks to Corollary
4.3.6
we obtain the functor (
3.1
).

Note that the functor (
3.1
) satisfies Property (E) from Sect.
3.1.4
holds. In fact,
the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
is t-exact by Sect.
4.1.6
.

4.3.10.

We are now ready to establish Property (B) (from Sect.
3.1.4
).
Indeed, it follows from Proposition
4.2.5
, since the spectral action
is determined by the Hecke functors (see
[
AGKRRV1
, Corollary 12.8.4(b)]
).

4.4.
Properties (C) and (D) of the specialization functor

4.4.1.

Note that the construction in
[
GLC1
, Sect. 3.3]
(see Sect.
7.1.1
below for a review)
makes sense over
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}
, and produces an object

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}).

In Sect.
7.1
we will prove:

Theorem 4.4.2
.

The object
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
is ULA over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

4.4.3.

By combining Theorem
4.4.2
with Proposition
4.3.3
and Lemma
4.1.9
,
we obtain Property (C).

4.4.4.

Consider the stacks

\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

and the projection

\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-}:\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}.

In Sect.
6.3
we will prove:

Theorem 4.4.5
.

The object

j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

is ULA with respect to the projection
\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-}
.

4.4.6.

By combining Theorem
4.4.5
and Lemma
4.1.9
we deduce Property (D)
of (
3.1
).

4.5.
Specialization and temperedness

In this subsection and formulate and prove a property of the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}

that describes its interaction with the
temperization
functor.

4.5.1.

We claim:

Proposition 4.5.2
.

The functor

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})

induces a functor

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}

so that the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})@>{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\\
@V{{\mathbf{u}}^{R}}V{}V@V{}V{{\mathbf{u}}^{R}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}\end{CD}

commutes.

We will give two proofs of this proposition.

4.5.3.
First proof

Let
x\in X
be a chosen point (which we use to define
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}
) and let

\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x}
is the spherical Hecke category at
x
(see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 1.5]
).

Recall (see
[
AG1
, Sect. 18.4]
) that the colocalization

{\mathbf{u}}^{R}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}:{\mathbf{u}}

can be characterized in terms of the action of the monoidal category

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{pt}\underset{{\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}}{\times}\operatorname{pt}/\operatorname{Ad}({\check{G}}))

on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, obtained as a composition of the

derived Satake
equivalence

\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}:={\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{pt}\underset{{\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}}{\times}\operatorname{pt}/\operatorname{Ad}({\check{G}}))\overset{\operatorname{Sat}_{G}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x}

and the natural action of
\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x}
on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
by Hecke functors.

Thus, in order to prove Proposition
4.5.2
, it suffices to show that the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}

intertwines the actions of
\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}
on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})
and on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
,
where we let
x
be a section
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})\to X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
.

By construction, the functor

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}:\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{K}}}\to\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{k}}}

intertwines the action of
\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{K}}}
on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})
with the action of

\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{k}}}
on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})
.

Hence, it remains to show that the endomorphism
\phi
of
\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}
that makes the
following diagram commute

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}@>{\phi}>{}>\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}\\
@V{\operatorname{Sat}_{G}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\operatorname{Sat}_{G}}V\\
\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{K}}}@>{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}>{}>\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{k}}}\end{CD}

is actually an automorphism.

We note that
\phi
has the following properties:

•

It is monoidal;

•

It makes the diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})@>{\operatorname{id}}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}@>{\phi}>{}>\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}\end{CD}

commute (this follows from Theorem
4.2.2
);

•

It induces the identity map on endomorphisms of the unit object

\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2])^{\check{G}}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}})\overset{\phi}{\to}{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2])^{\check{G}}.

The third points follow from the fact that the composite

\operatorname{Sym}({\mathfrak{t}}^{*}[-2])^{W}\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\operatorname{pt}/G_{\mathsf{K}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{K}}}})\overset{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x,{\mathsf{k}}}})\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\operatorname{pt}/G_{\mathsf{k}})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}({\mathfrak{t}}^{*}[-2])^{W}

is the identity map, while the identification
\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2])^{\check{G}}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}})
equals

\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2])^{\check{G}}\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(\check{\mathfrak{t}}[-2])^{W}\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{Sym}({\mathfrak{t}}^{*}[-2])^{W}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Sph}(G)_{x}})\overset{\operatorname{Sat}_{G}}{\simeq}{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}}).

We now claim:

Lemma 4.5.4
.

Any endomorphism of
\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}
that has the above three properties is an automorphism.

The proof of the lemma is given in Sect.
4.5.6
below.

∎[First proof of Proposition
4.5.2
]

4.5.5.
Second proof

We have to show that the functor
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
sends

\operatorname{ker}({\mathbf{u}}^{R}):\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})\overset{{\mathbf{u}}^{R}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}

to

\operatorname{ker}({\mathbf{u}}^{R}):\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\overset{{\mathbf{u}}^{R}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{temp}}.

Recall the characterization of the kernel of

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbf{u}}^{R}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{temp}}

in
[
FR
, Sect. 4.3]
.

The required assertion follows from the fact that the functors involved in this characterization commute with

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
.

∎[Second proof of Proposition
4.5.2
]

4.5.6.
Proof of Lemma
4.5.4

We rewrite

\operatorname{Sph}^{\operatorname{spec}}({\check{G}})_{x}\simeq\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]){\operatorname{-mod}}^{{\check{G}}}.

Using the first two properties, we can de-equivariantize both sides, i.e.,
apply
\operatorname{Vect}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})}{\otimes}-
, and obtain a functor

\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]){\operatorname{-mod}}\overset{\widetilde{\phi}}{\to}\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]){\operatorname{-mod}}

with the following properties:

•

It is
{\check{G}}
-equivariant;

•

It sends
\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2])\in\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]){\operatorname{-mod}}
to itself;

•

It makes the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2])^{\check{G}}@>{\operatorname{id}}>{}>\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2])^{\check{G}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2])&&\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2])\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{\sim}V{}V\\
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]){\operatorname{-mod}}}(\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]))@>{\widetilde{\phi}}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]){\operatorname{-mod}}}(\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]))\end{CD}

commute.

It suffices to show that the bottom horizontal arrow in this diagram is an isomorphism. This arrow is a map
of algebras, hence is determined by a map of vector spaces.

{\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]\to\operatorname{Sym}({\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}[-2]).

The grading forces the latter map to come from a map of vector spaces
{\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}\to{\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
. By
{\check{G}}
-equivariance,
the latter map acts as a scalar on each simple factor.

However, the commutation of the above diagram forces this scalar to be
1
.

∎[Lemma
4.5.4
]

5.
Proof of Property (A)

The goal of this section is to establish Property (A) of the functor (
3.1
).

5.1.
The key input

5.1.1.

Consider the object

(5.1)

(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}).

5.1.2.

The key input in the proof of Property (A) is provided by the following result:

Theorem 5.1.3
.

The object (
5.1
) is ULA over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

The theorem will be proved in Sect.
6.4
. We now proceed with the proof of Property (A).

5.1.4.

Combining Theorem
5.1.3
with Lemma
4.1.9
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.1.5
.

The natural map

(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})\simeq(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})_{!}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})

is an isomorphism.

Using the commutation of specialization with Verdier duality, from Corollary
5.1.5
we obtain:

Corollary 5.1.6
.

The canonical map

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\to(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\simeq(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})

is an isomorphism.

5.2.
Harder-Narasimhan strata

5.2.1.

Recall the notion of
contractive
locally closed substack, see
[
DG1
, Sect. 5.2.1]
.

For
\theta\in\Lambda^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, let

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta)}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

be the open union of Harder-Narasimhan strata, as defined in
[
DG1
, Sect. 7.3.4]
.

The main technical result of the paper
[
DG1
]
, proved in Sect. 9.3 of
loc. cit.
, says that for all
\theta
large enough
and
\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta
, the (locally) closed substack

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta^{\prime})}-\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta)}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta^{\prime})}

is contractive.

The proof of this result applies equally well in the relative situation over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

5.2.2.

Let
\theta\leq\theta^{\prime}
be as above and consider the corresponding open embedding

\jmath_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}}:\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta)}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta^{\prime})}.

We are going to prove:

Proposition 5.2.3
.

The natural transformations

(\jmath_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}})_{!}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\jmath_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}})_{!}

and

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\jmath_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}})_{*}\to(\jmath_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

We will prove the first isomorphism, as the second one is similar.

It is enough to prove the assertion after applying pullback with respect to a smooth surjective map.
Hence, by the definition of contractiveness, it is enough to prove the more general Proposition
5.2.5

below.

∎

5.2.4.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
be as in
[
DG1
, Sect. 5.1.1]
. Denote

{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}:={\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}-{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{\jmath}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}.

Proposition 5.2.5
.

The natural transformation

\jmath_{!}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\jmath_{!}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
\imath
denote the closed embedding
{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
. It is enough to show that
the natural transformation

(5.2)

\imath^{*}\circ\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\imath^{*},

as functors
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathsf{K}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathsf{k}})
,
is an isomorphism.

As in
[
DG1
, Sects. 5.1.3-5.1.5]
, we can assume that
15
15

15

In the formulas below,
\operatorname{pt}:=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
, and similarly
for
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
and
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

{\mathcal{Z}}=\operatorname{pt}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times Z
and

{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathbb{A}}^{n}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times Z
for some base
Z
, where
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acts on
{\mathbb{A}}^{n}
via the

m
th power of the standard character, where
m>0
. Further, applying blow-up
(see
[
DG1
, Sect. 5.1.6]
), we can assume that
n=1
.

It is enough to prove that the map (
5.2
) is an isomorphism on the generators.
We take the generators to be of the form

(f_{m})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{Z},

where

•

f_{m}
is the “raising to the power
m
” map
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
;

•

{\mathcal{F}}_{Z}\in\operatorname{Shv}(Z)
,

•

{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
is either
\delta_{0,{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
or
\mathsf{e}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
.

In the above cases, the fact that (
5.2
) is an isomorphism is evident.

∎

5.3.
Specialization for the “co”-category

5.3.1.

Recall the category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
, see
[
AGKRRV2
, Sects. 2.5 and C.2]
. By definition,

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\underset{\theta\in\Lambda^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta)}),

where the colimit is taken with respect to the functors
(\jmath_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}})_{*}
.

From Proposition
5.2.3
we obtain that there exists a well-defined functor

(5.3)

\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}

that makes the following diagrams commute

(5.4)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}\\
@A{(\jmath_{\theta})_{\operatorname{co,*}}}A{}A@A{}A{(\jmath_{\theta})_{\operatorname{co,*}}}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}^{(\leq\theta)})@>{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}^{(\leq\theta)}),\end{CD}

where:

•

\jmath_{\theta}
denotes the open embedding
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta)}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
;

•

(\jmath_{\theta})_{\operatorname{co,*}}
denotes the corresponding tautological functor
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta)})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

(not to be confused with
(\jmath_{\theta})_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta)})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

5.3.2.

Moreover, the following diagram commutes tautologically

(5.5)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}\\
@V{}V{\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})@>{\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}),\end{CD}

where

\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is as in
[
AGKRRV2
, Sect. C.2.3]
.

5.3.3.

Recall that for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}
, its Verdier dual is well-defined as an object

{\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

The commutation of specialization with Verdier duality implies that for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})^{c}

we have a canonical isomorphism

(5.6)

\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq{\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}}(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}))

as objects in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

5.3.4.

We now consider the “mixed” category
