\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}},

defined in
[
AGKRRV2
, Sect. C.4.2]
.

By definition,

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}=\underset{\theta\in\Lambda^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}^{(\leq\theta)}),

where the colimit is taken with respect to the functors

(\operatorname{id}\times\jmath_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}})_{*}
.

According to
[
AGKRRV2
, Sect. C.4.3]
, the functor

(5.7)

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}\to\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

is an equivalence, where:

•

{\mathcal{U}}
runs over the poset of quasi-compact open substacks of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
;

•

For
{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
, the functor
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

is given by restriction.

The discussion in Sect.
5.3.1
applies equally to the mixed situation.

In addition, we have a tautologically defined functor

\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

and the corresponding counterpart of diagram (
5.5
) commutes.

5.3.5.

We will consider the corresponding functor

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}.

5.3.6.

Recall now the object

(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}},

defined in
[
AGKRRV2
, Sect. C.4.6]
.

In terms of the equivalence (
5.7
), the restriction of
(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})

to a given
{\mathcal{U}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is

(\operatorname{id}\times\jmath)_{\operatorname{co},*}\circ(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}})_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{U}}),\quad\jmath:{\mathcal{U}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Bun}_{G}.

We have

\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}))\simeq(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}),

as objects of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

5.3.7.

We are going to prove:

Proposition 5.3.8
.

There exist a unique isomorphism

(5.8)

\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\right)\simeq(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})

as objects of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}
that makes the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}\circ\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\right)@>{\sim}>{\text{\eqref{e:Sp omega fine}}}>\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}\circ(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})@>{\sim}>{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Sp diag omega}}}>(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})\end{CD}

commute.

Proof.

Using the equivalence (
5.7
), we have to show that there exists a unique family
of isomorphisms

(5.9)

\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\right)|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathsf{k}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}}\simeq(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathsf{k}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}}

as objects of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathsf{k}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}
, compatible via
\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}
with the isomorphism

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathsf{k}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}}\simeq(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathsf{k}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}},

induced by the isomorphism of Corollary
5.1.6
, where
{\mathcal{U}}
runs over the poset of quasi-compact open substacks of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

We can replace the poset of all
{\mathcal{U}}
by a cofinal one that consists of the substacks

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq\theta)}
for
\theta
sufficiently large.

The left-hand side in (
5.9
) is by definition

(5.10)

\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times\jmath_{\theta})_{\operatorname{co},*}\circ(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}^{(\leq\theta)}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}^{(\leq\theta)}}).

Using (
5.4
), we write it as

(5.11)

(\operatorname{id}\times\jmath_{\theta})_{\operatorname{co},*}\left(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\circ(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}^{(\leq\theta)}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}^{(\leq\theta)}})\right).

The right-hand in (
5.9
) is

(5.12)

(\operatorname{id}\times\jmath_{\theta})_{\operatorname{co},*}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}^{(\leq\theta)}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}^{(\leq\theta)}})\right).

By Corollary
5.1.6
, the expressions in (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) become isomorphic after applying the functor

\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}^{(\leq\theta)}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}^{(\leq\theta)}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}).

Now, the required assertion follows from the fact that the functor
\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}
is fully faithful on the essential image of

(\operatorname{id}\times\jmath_{\theta})_{\operatorname{co},*}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}^{(\leq\theta)}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}^{(\leq\theta)})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}^{(\leq\theta)}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}.

∎

5.4.
Method of proof

In this subsection we launch the proof of Property (A) proper.

5.4.1.

The proof is based on the following principle:

Let
F:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
be a functor between compactly generated
categories. Assume that
F
preserves compactness, so it admits a continuous right adjoint, denoted
F^{R}
.
Denote

F^{\operatorname{op}}:=(F^{R})^{\vee},\quad{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\vee}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{\vee}.

Explicitly, identifying

{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}_{i}:=\operatorname{Ind}(({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}),

the functor
F^{\operatorname{op}}
is obtained by ind-extending the same-named functor on compact objects.

5.4.2.

Consider the tautological map

(5.13)

(F\otimes F^{\operatorname{op}})({\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{1}})\to{\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{2}},

where
{\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i}}\in{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}_{i}
is the unit of the self-duality.

The map (
5.13
) is characterized as follows. For
{\mathbf{c}}_{1}\in{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{c}
with formal
dual
{\mathbf{c}}_{1}^{\vee}\in{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\vee}
, the diagram

(5.14)

\begin{CD}(F\otimes F^{\operatorname{op}})({\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{1}})@>{\text{\eqref{e:map of units}}}>{}>{\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{2}}\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
(F\otimes F^{\operatorname{op}})({\mathbf{c}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{c}}_{1}^{\vee})@>{\sim}>{}>F({\mathbf{c}}_{1})\otimes F({\mathbf{c}}_{1})^{\vee},\end{CD}

commutes, where the vertical arrows are the canonical maps

(5.15)

\operatorname{can}_{\mathbf{c}}:{\mathbf{c}}\otimes{\mathbf{c}}^{\vee}\to u_{{\mathbf{C}}},\quad{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{c}

for a compactly generated category
{\mathbf{C}}
.

Remark 5.4.3
.

Properly speaking, the object
u_{{\mathbf{C}}}\in{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
can be characterized as the colimit
over the the category of twisted arrows in
{\mathbf{C}}^{c}
that sends

(\phi:{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime\prime}\to{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime})\in\operatorname{TwArr}({\mathbf{C}}^{c})\,\mapsto({\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}\otimes({\mathbf{c}}^{\prime\prime})^{\vee})\in{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}.

Indeed, for
\phi:{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime\prime}\to{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}
as above, the corresponding map
{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}\otimes({\mathbf{c}}^{\prime\prime})^{\vee}\to u_{\mathbf{C}}

is either of the circuits in the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}\otimes({\mathbf{c}}^{\prime\prime})^{\vee}@>{\operatorname{id}\otimes\phi^{\vee}}>{}>{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}\otimes({\mathbf{c}}^{\prime})^{\vee}\\
@V{\phi\otimes\operatorname{id}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{can}_{{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}}}V\\
{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime\prime}\otimes({\mathbf{c}}^{\prime\prime})^{\vee}@>{\operatorname{can}_{{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime\prime}}}>{}>u_{{\mathbf{C}}}.\end{CD}

In what follows, we have chosen to simplify the exposition by not explicitly
mentioning the twisted arrows functoriality and working only with a single object at a
time. To make the discussion complete, one replaces the eventual reference to
(
4.9
)
with a reference to Remark
4.1.7
.

5.4.4.

We have:

Lemma 5.4.5
.

The functor
F
is a Verdier quotient if and only if the map (
5.13
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We need to show that the counit of the adjunction

F\circ F^{R}\to\operatorname{Id}

is an isomorphism. I.e., we have to show that the map

(5.16)

((F\circ F^{R})\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{2}})\to{\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{2}}

is an isomorphism.

Note that
F^{R}
identifies with the functor dual to
F^{\operatorname{op}}
. Hence, we can rewrite

((F\circ F^{R})\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{2}})=(F\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ(F^{R}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ({\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{2}})\simeq(F\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ((F^{\operatorname{op}})^{\vee}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ({\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{2}})\simeq\\
\simeq(F\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes F^{\operatorname{op}})({\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{1}})=(F\otimes F^{\operatorname{op}})({\mathbf{u}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{1}}).

Under this identification, the map (
5.16
) becomes the map (
5.13
).

∎

5.4.6.

We will show that the situation described above takes place for

{\mathbf{C}}_{1}:=\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}),\,\,{\mathbf{C}}_{1}:=\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}),\,\,F=\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}.

5.4.7.

First, recall that the functor (
3.1
) preserves compactness, see Sect.
3.1.7

(this relies on Properties (B), (C) and (D) , which are proved independently of Property (A)).

Next, recall that (when working over a field), according to
[
AGKRRV2
, Sects. 2.5.8, Corollary 2.6.5 and Proposition 2.7.6]
, the dual of the category

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
identifies with

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

in such a way that the unit of the duality is the object

(5.17)

{\mathsf{P}}((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})),

where:

•

{\mathsf{P}}
denotes here Beilinson’s projector, applied along the first factor;

•

The object in (
5.17
), which a priori lies in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}

belongs in fact to

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}.

5.4.8.

Furthermore (still working over a field), the corresponding equivalence

(\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq(\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}})^{c}

is induced by the Verdier duality equivalence

(\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq(\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}})^{c},

where we are using the fact that the embedidng

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}

preserves compactness, by Theorem
1.1.7
.

5.4.9.

A variant of the above discussion applies to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})
, where we only need to replace

{\mathsf{P}}
by
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
.

5.4.10.

We claim that the functor
\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}
of (
5.3
) sends

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}

and identifies with
(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}})^{\operatorname{op}}
(in the notations of Sect.
5.4.1
).

Indeed, this follows by combining (
5.6
) and Sect.
5.4.8
.

5.4.11.

Thus, by Lemma
5.4.5

it remains to show that the map (
5.13
), which in our case is the map

(5.18)

(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\otimes\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}})\left({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}))\right)\to{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{k}}}((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})),

is an isomorphism in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

5.5.
Verification

5.5.1.

Applying the (fully faithful) functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\\
\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}},

it suffices to show that (
5.18
) is an isomorphism of objects in

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}
.

5.5.2.

By Proposition
4.3.3
, we identify the left-hand side in (
5.18
) with

{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\right),

and using Proposition
5.3.8
, we identify it further with

{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})\right),

which is the right-hand side in (
5.18
).

It remains to show that the map in (
5.18
) corresponds under the above
identifications to the identity map.

5.5.3.

By (
5.14
), we have to show that for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})^{c}
, the isomorphism

(5.19)

\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}))\right)\overset{\text{ Proposition~\ref{p:Sp and P}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{k}}}\circ\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\right)\overset{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:Sp omega fine}}}{\simeq}{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})\right)

makes the following diagram commute

(5.20)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}))\right)@>{\text{\eqref{e:map of units to verify 1}}}>{}>{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})\right)\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathcal{F}}))@>{\sim}>{}>\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}}(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})),\end{CD}

where:

•

The left vertical arrow is

(5.21)

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathcal{F}}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:Sp Kunneth}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathcal{F}}))\to\\
\to\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left({\mathsf{P}}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}))\right),

where the last arrow is obtained by applying
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
to the canonical map (
5.15
), which in our case is

(5.22)

{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathcal{F}})\to(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}});

•

The right vertical arrow is the map (
5.15
).

5.5.4.

Recall now that due to the validity of Theorem
1.1.7
, the functor
{\mathsf{P}}
is the right adjoint of the embedding

\operatorname{emb.Nilp}
, see
[
AGKRRV1
, Proposition 17.2.3]
.

From here, we formally obtain that the functor
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
provides a right adjoint to the embedding

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}).

We obtain that the commutation of diagram (
5.20
)
is equivalent to the commutation of the following diagram:

(5.23)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\right)@>{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:Sp omega fine}}}>{}>(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathcal{F}}))@>{\sim}>{}>\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}}(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})),\end{CD}

where:

•

The left vertical arrow is

(5.24)

\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathcal{F}}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:Sp Kunneth}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathcal{F}}))\to\\
\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}),

where the last arrow is obtained by applying
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathsf{K}},{\mathsf{k}}}
to the canonical map (
5.26
) below;

•

The right vertical arrow is the map (
5.26
).

5.5.5.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an algebraic stack. For an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
there exists a canonical map

(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\to{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}).

Passing to Verdier duals, we obtain a map

(5.25)

{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}})\to(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}).

5.5.6.

We take
{\mathcal{Y}}=\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
and
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}
. In this case, the map (
5.25
)
lifts canonically to a map

(5.26)

{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}})\to(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})^{\operatorname{fine}}_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})

in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}
.

Indeed, both sides in (
5.26
) belong to the essential image of

(\operatorname{id}\times\jmath)_{\operatorname{co},*}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times{\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}

for a quasi-compact
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{\jmath}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, cf. proof of Proposition
5.3.8
.

5.5.7.

Now, it follows by Verdier duality from (
4.9
) that the diagram

(5.27)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}_{2}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}\left((\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}})\right)@>{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:Sp omega fine}}}>{}>(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}})\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Sp}^{\operatorname{co}}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{K}}}}({\mathcal{F}}))@>{\sim}>{}>\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}}(\operatorname{Sp}_{{\mathsf{K}}\to{\mathsf{k}}}({\mathcal{F}}))\end{CD}

obtained from (
5.23
) by applying

\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{k}}})

does commute.

This formally implies the commutation of (
5.23
) by the same principle as in the proof
of Proposition
5.3.8
.

6.
Proofs of the local acyclicity theorems

The goal of this section is to prove the ULA theorems stated in the previous section.

6.1.
Proof of Theorem
4.2.2

6.1.1.

Let
\Delta^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}},\nabla^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})
be the standard and costandard objects corresponding to
\lambda
,
respectively. I.e., they are, respectively, the !- and *- extensions of the (cohomologically shifted)
constant sheaf on
\operatorname{Hecke}^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
.

It is enough to show that
\Delta^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
and
\nabla^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

are ULA over
\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
.
Indeed, this would imply that
\operatorname{IC}^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}:=\operatorname{IC}_{\overline{\operatorname{Hecke}}^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}
is also ULA and has the specified
restrictions over
{\mathsf{k}}
(this follows from the fact that the ULA condition is inherited by the passage to subquotients of perverse cohomologies,
see
[
HS
, Corollary 1.12]
).

We will prove the assertion for
\Delta^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
; the assertion for
\nabla^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
will follow by duality.

6.1.2.

Let
\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
be the moduli stack whose
S
-points are:

•

An
S
-point
x
of
X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
;

•

A
G
-bundle
{\mathcal{P}}
on
X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}S
;

•

A reduction to
B
of the restriction of
{\mathcal{P}}
along

S\overset{(x,\operatorname{id})}{\to}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}S
.

Consider the fiber product

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}:=\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}},

equipped with the maps

\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\leftarrow}{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}}{\to}\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}.

It is naturally stratified by locally closed subsets

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}^{\lambda^{\prime}},\quad\lambda^{\prime}\in\Lambda\simeq W\backslash W^{\operatorname{aff,ext}},

where
W^{\operatorname{aff,ext}}
is the extended affine Weyl group. Denote by
{}^{\prime}\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
the corresponding
standard object.

The pullback of
\Delta^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
along the (smooth) projection

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\to\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

admits a filtration with subquotients
{}^{\prime}\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
for

\lambda^{\prime}\in\Lambda
projecting to
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\simeq\Lambda/W
.

Hence, order to prove that
\Delta^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
is ULA over
\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
(with respect to
both
\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}
and
\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}
), it suffices to show that the objects
{}^{\prime}\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
are ULA over

\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
with respect to
{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}
and over
\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
with respect to
{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}
.

6.1.3.

Let
\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
be the moduli stack
16
16

16

Here “Fl” stands for “affine flags”, as opposed to the usual
Hecke stack, which is modeled on the affine Grassmannian.
whose
S
-points are:

•

An
S
-point
x
of
X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
;

•

A pair of
G
-bundles
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
on
X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}S
;

•

Reductions to
B
of the restrictions of
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
along

S\overset{(x,\operatorname{id})}{\to}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}S
;

•

An isomorphism
{\mathcal{P}}|_{X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}S-S}\simeq{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}|_{X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}S-S}
.

Denote by
\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}^{\operatorname{Fl}}
and
\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{\operatorname{Fl}}
the natural projections

\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}^{\operatorname{Fl}}}{\leftarrow}\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{\operatorname{Fl}}}{\to}\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}.

The prestack
\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
is an ind-algebraic stack. It is naturally stratified by locally closed
substacks
\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
, where
\widetilde{w}
runs over
W^{\operatorname{aff,ext}}
.

For a given
\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}
, let

\Delta^{\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

denote the corresponding standard object
(i.e., the !-extension of the constant sheaf on
\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
).

6.1.4.

We have a Cartesian diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}@>{}>{}>{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})\\
@V{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{\operatorname{Fl}}}V{}V@V{}V{{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}}V\\
\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\end{CD}

with the horizontal maps being smooth and proper.

The object
{}^{\prime}\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Hecke}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}))
is isomorphic (up to a cohomological shift)
to the direct image of
\Delta^{\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})
for any
\widetilde{w}\in W^{\operatorname{aff,ext}}

that projects to
\lambda^{\prime}
under
W^{\operatorname{aff,ext}}\to W\backslash W^{\operatorname{aff,ext}}\simeq\Lambda
.

Hence, it is enough to show that the objects
\Delta^{\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
are ULA over
\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

for both
\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}^{\operatorname{Fl}}
and
\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{\operatorname{Fl}}
.

6.1.5.

We argue by induction on the length
\ell(\widetilde{w})
.

When
\ell(\widetilde{w})=0
, the map

\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s\text{ or}\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

is an isomorphism, and there is nothing to prove.

When
\ell(\widetilde{w})=1
, the map

\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s\text{ or}\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

is smooth fibration with fibers isomorphic to
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
. Similarly, the closure

\overline{\operatorname{Hecke}}{}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\supset\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

is a smooth fibration

\overline{\operatorname{Hecke}}{}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s\text{ or}\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

with fibers isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
.

Hence, the object
\Delta^{\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
is a cone of a map between constant sheaves on schemes that are smooth
over
\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
. Hence, it is ULA.

6.1.6.

For
\widetilde{w}
of length
\geq 2
, choose a decomposition

\widetilde{w}=\widetilde{w}_{1}\cdot\widetilde{w}_{2},\quad\ell(\widetilde{w})=\ell(\widetilde{w}_{1})+\ell(\widetilde{w}_{2}).

Consider the convolution diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}.}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{},\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s}
\scriptstyle{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s}
\scriptstyle{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s}
\scriptstyle{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s}
\scriptstyle{{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}
\scriptstyle{{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}}

By the induction hypothesis, the object

(6.1)

\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{1}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{2}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}:=({}^{\prime}\!\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{})^{*}(\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{1}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})\overset{*}{\otimes}({}^{\prime}\!\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{})^{*}(\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{2}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{},\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

is ULA with respect to both

(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s)\circ{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\text{ and }(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s)\circ{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}.

6.1.7.

Consider the convolution map

\operatorname{conv}:\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{},\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\to\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})}{\times}X_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

so that

(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s)\circ{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}=(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s)\circ\operatorname{conv}\text{ and }(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s)\circ{}^{\prime}\!\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}=(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s)\circ\operatorname{conv}.

We obtain that

\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{1}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{2}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}

is ULA with respect to both

(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s)\circ\operatorname{conv}\text{ and }(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s)\circ\operatorname{conv}.

Since the map
\operatorname{conv}
is proper, we obtain that

\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{1}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\star\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{2}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}:=\operatorname{conv}_{!}(\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{1}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{2}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

is ULA with respect to both
\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s
and
\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s
.

6.1.8.

Now, since the map
\operatorname{conv}
induces an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}_{1}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\underset{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{},\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{Fl}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}_{2}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\to\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{Fl},\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}},

we obtain that

\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{1}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\star\Delta^{\widetilde{w_{2}}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\simeq\Delta^{\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}.

Hence,
\Delta^{\widetilde{w}}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
is also ULA as required.

∎[Theorem
4.2.2
]

6.2.
The key mechanism

We now proceed with the proofs of Theorems
4.4.5
and
5.1.3
. The proof will
be based on the
contraction principle
, embodied by Proposition
6.2.2
below.

6.2.1.

We place ourselves again in the situation of

[
DG2
]
, over an arbitrary Noetherian base
S
(i.e., this is a generalization of the context of Sect.
5.2.4
,
where instead of
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
we have a more general
S
).

We claim:

Proposition 6.2.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
be ULA over
S
. Then so
is
j_{!}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

Proof.

Repeats the proof of Proposition
5.2.5
.

∎

Remark 6.2.3
.

The assertion of Proposition
6.2.2
replicates that of
[
HHS
, Theorem 6.1.3]
17
17

17

We are grateful to L. Hamann
for pointing this out to us.
. We refer the reader to
loc. cit.
where the proof is written out in detail.

Remark 6.2.4
.

As a side remark, we observe that Proposition
6.2.2
allows us to give an alternative proof of Theorem
4.2.2
:

We can show that the objects
\Delta^{\lambda}_{X,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
are ULA by reducing
to a contractive situation as in the original Kazhdan-Lusztig paper
[
KL
]
,
by intersecting with the opposite Schubert strata.

6.3.
Proof of Theorem
4.4.5

The proof below is inspired by the computation of IC stalks in
[
BFGM
]
, and follows the same
line of thought as that in
[
HHS
, Theorem 6.2.1]
.

6.3.1.

Let
M
denote the Levi quotient of
P^{-}
. Let
J\subset I
be the subset of the Dynkin diagram of
G

that corresponds to the roots inside
M
.

Denote by
\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}_{G,P}
the monoid equal to
the non-negative integral span of
\alpha_{i}
,
i\in(I-J)
.
We endow it with the standard order relation.

Recall (see
[
BG1
, Sect. 1.3.3]
) that
\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}
carries a stratification indexed by elements of
\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}_{G,P}
,

\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}=\underset{\theta\in\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}_{G,P}}{\cup}\,(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})_{\theta},

with the open stratum
(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})_{0}
being
\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}
.

For a given
\theta
, let

(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})_{<\theta}\subset\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}\text{ and }(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})_{\leq\theta}\subset\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}

be the open substacks

\underset{\theta^{\prime}<\theta}{\cup}\,(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})_{\theta^{\prime}}\text{ and }\underset{\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta}{\cup}\,(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})_{\theta^{\prime}},

respectively.

We will prove Theorem
4.4.5
by induction on
\theta
. The base of the induction is when
\theta=0
, in which case

(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})_{\leq\theta}=\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}
, which is smooth over
\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
, and the ULA property
18
18

18

For the duration
of this proof, “ULA” means “ULA over
\operatorname{Bun}_{M,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
”.
is obvious.

Thus, we will assume that the ULA statement holds for

j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{<\theta}}

and we will deduce its validity for

j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{\leq\theta}}.

6.3.2.

Consider the parabolic Zastava space
\operatorname{Zast}
, see Sect.
9.3.7
below. Pulling back the above stratification along
the projection

\operatorname{Zast}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}},

we obtain a stratification on
\operatorname{Zast}
. We denote by

\operatorname{Zast}_{\theta},\,\,\operatorname{Zast}_{\leq\theta},\,\,\operatorname{Zast}_{<\theta}

the corresponding substacks.

Denote

\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}:=\operatorname{Zast}_{0}=\operatorname{Zast}\underset{\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}.

Denote by
j_{\operatorname{Zast}}
the open embedding
\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Zast}
.

Recall also that
\operatorname{Zast}
splits as a disjoint union

\operatorname{Zast}:=\underset{\theta\in\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}_{G,P}}{\sqcup}\,\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}.

6.3.3.

Arguing as in
[
BFGM
, Sect. 5]
or
[
Ga4
, Sect. 3.9]
, we obtain:

Lemma 6.3.4
.

(a)
The ULA property of
j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{<\theta}}

implies the ULA property of

(j_{\operatorname{Zast}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\operatorname{Zast}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{<\theta}}.

(b) The following statements are equivalent:

(i)
j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{\leq\theta}}
is ULA;

(ii)
(j_{\operatorname{Zast}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\operatorname{Zast}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{\leq\theta}}
is ULA;

(iii)
(j_{\operatorname{Zast}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{\leq\theta}}
is ULA.

6.3.5.

Let us denote by
j_{<\theta,\leq\theta}
the open embedding

\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{<\theta}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{\leq\theta}.

Note that the complement of this embedding is the closed stratum
\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{\theta}\subset\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{\leq\theta}
.

Thus, we have to show

(6.2)

(j_{\operatorname{Zast}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{<\theta}}\text{ is ULA }\Rightarrow(j_{<\theta,\leq\theta})_{!}\left((j_{\operatorname{Zast}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{<\theta}}\right)\text{ is ULA }.

6.3.6.

Recall now (see
[
BFGM
, Sect. 5.1]
) that
\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}=\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{\leq\theta}
carries an action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

that contracts it onto the locus
\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{\theta}
.

Since the object
(j_{\operatorname{Zast}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}})|_{(\operatorname{Zast}^{\theta}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{<\theta}}

is
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariant, the implication (
6.2
) follows from Proposition
6.2.2
.

∎[Theorem
4.4.5
]

6.4.
Proof of Theorem
5.1.3

It is easy to reduce the assertion to the case when
G
is semi-simple, which we will now assume.
19
19

19

Otherwise
replace
R_{Z_{G}}
below by the
!
-direct image of
\mathsf{e}
along
the map
\operatorname{pt}\to\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G}
.

6.4.1.

Recall that according to
[
Sch
]
, the diagonal map

\Delta:\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

can be factored as

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}=\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{pt}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}\overset{\overline{\Delta}}{\to}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G},

where the map
j
is an open embedding and
\overline{\Delta}
is proper.

6.4.2.
Example

For
G=SL_{2}
, the stack
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}
classifies triples

({\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{2},\alpha)/{\mathbb{G}}_{m},

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
rank-2 bundles with trivialized determinants,
and
\alpha
is a
non-zero
map
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
.
The action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
is given by scaling
\alpha
.

The open locus
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G}
corresponds to the condition that
\alpha
be an isomorphism.

6.4.3.

Thus, it suffices to show that the object

(6.3)

j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}\boxtimes R_{Z_{G}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

is ULA over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
, where

R_{Z_{G}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(Z_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})/Z_{G})

denotes the regular representation.

6.4.4.

Recall (see
[
Sch
, Sect. 2.2.7]
) that stack
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}
admits a stratification indexed by the poset of standard parabolics in
G
:

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}=\underset{P}{\cup}\,\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P}

with the open stratum
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,G}
being the image of the embedding
j
.

Denote by
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,\geq P}
(resp.,
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,>P}
) the open substack equal to the union of the strata
corresponding to the parabolics
P^{\prime}
with
P\subset P^{\prime}
(resp.,
P\subsetneq P^{\prime}
).

We argue by induction and assume that the object (
6.3
) is ULA when restricted to the open substack

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,>P,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
. We now perform the induction step and prove that the ULA property holds over

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,\geq P,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
.

6.4.5.

Consider the stratum
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P}
. According to
[
Sch
, Sect. 3]
that
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P}
admits a further stratification
indexed by elements of
\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}_{G,P}
:

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P}=\underset{\theta\in\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}_{G,P}}{\cup}\,(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P})_{\theta}.

For a given
\theta
, let

(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P})_{<\theta}\subset(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P})_{\leq\theta}

be the corresponding open subsets.

In particular, we have the open locus

(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P})_{0}\subset\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P}.

6.4.6.
Example

In the example of
G=SL_{2}
, the Borel stratum corresponds to the condition that the map
\alpha

has generic rank
1
and hence factors as

{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\overset{\beta_{1}}{\twoheadrightarrow}{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\overset{\gamma}{\to}{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\overset{\beta_{2}}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}_{2},

where
{\mathcal{L}}_{i}
are line bundles and
\beta_{i}
are bundle maps.

The stratification by
\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}_{G,P}={\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0}
is given by the total degree of zeroes of the map
\gamma
.

6.4.7.

Denote by
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,\geq P,<\theta}
and
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,\geq P,\leq\theta}
the open substacks

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,>P}\cup(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P})_{<\theta}\text{ and }\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,>P}\cup(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,P})_{\leq\theta},

respectively.

By induction, we can assume that (
6.3
) is ULA
20
20

20

For the duration of this proof, “ULA” means “ULA over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

when restricted to
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,\geq P,<\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
. We will
now perform the induction step and prove that the ULA property holds over
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,\geq P,\leq\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
.

Remark 6.4.8
.

The rest of the argument, which is explained below, uses the same principle as the proof of Theorem
4.4.5
: we will
replace
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,\geq P,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
by its local model and reduce the assertion to a contractive situation
(i.e., one covered by Proposition
6.2.2
).

6.5.
Proof of Theorem
5.1.3
, continuation

6.5.1.

Let
Y^{P}
denote the open substack

\left(\operatorname{Bun}_{P}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}\right)^{\operatorname{tr}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{P}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}},

where the superscript “tr” refers to the generic transversality condition, see
[
Sch
, Sect. 6.1.6]
.

6.5.2.
Example

For
G=SL_{2}
, the fiber product

\operatorname{Bun}_{P}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}

classifies the data of

{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{1}\overset{\delta_{1}}{\to}{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\overset{\alpha}{\to}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\overset{\delta_{2}}{\to}{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{2},

where
\delta_{i}
are bundle maps.

The generic transversality condition is that the composite map
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{1}\to{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{2}
be non-zero.

6.5.3.

The stratifications on
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G}
induce the corresponding stratifications on
Y^{P}
, to be denoted

Y^{P}_{P^{\prime}}
,
Y^{P}_{P^{\prime},\theta}
, etc. We note that
Y^{P}_{P^{\prime}}=\emptyset
unless
P\subset P^{\prime}
.

Note that the open stratum
Y^{P}_{G}
identifies with

\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}\times\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G}.

Denote by
j_{Y}
the open embedding

\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}\times\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G}\hookrightarrow Y^{P}.

In addition,
Y^{P}
splits as a disjoint union

(6.4)

Y^{P}:=\underset{\theta\in\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}_{G,P}}{\sqcup}\,Y^{P,\theta},

and
Y^{P,\theta}_{P,\theta^{\prime}}
is empty unless
\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta
.

6.5.4.
Example

In the example of
G=SL_{2}
, the decomposition (
6.4
) is
according to the total degree of zeroes of the map
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{1}\to{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{2}
.

6.5.5.

The following is parallel to Lemma
6.3.4
:

Lemma 6.5.6
.

(a) The ULA property of

j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}\boxtimes R_{Z_{G}})|_{\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,\geq P,<\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}

implies the ULA property of

(j_{Y})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}}\boxtimes R_{Z_{G}})|_{Y^{P}_{\geq P,<\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}.

(b) The following conditions are equivalent:

(i)
j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}\boxtimes R_{Z_{G}})|_{\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{G,\geq P,\leq\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}

is ULA;

(ii)
(j_{Y})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}}\boxtimes R_{Z_{G}})|_{Y^{P}_{\geq P,\leq\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}

is ULA;

(iii)
(j_{Y})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}}\boxtimes R_{Z_{G}})|_{Y^{P,\theta}_{\geq P,\leq\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}

is ULA.

6.5.7.

Let
j_{\geq P,<\theta,\leq\theta}
the open embedding

Y^{P,\theta}_{\geq P,<\theta}\hookrightarrow Y^{P,\theta}_{\geq P,\leq\theta}.

Note that the complement of this embedding is the closed stratum

Y^{P,\theta}_{P,\theta}\subset Y^{P,\theta}_{\geq P,\leq\theta}.

6.5.8.
Example

In the example of
G=SL_{2}
, the closed substack
Y^{P,\theta}_{P,\theta}
is the locus
where the maps

{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{1}\to{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\text{ and }{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{2}

are isomorphisms.

So
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}={\mathcal{L}}_{i}\oplus{\mathcal{L}}_{i}^{\otimes-1}
, and the map
\alpha
is

{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\simeq{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\oplus{\mathcal{L}}_{1}^{\otimes-1}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\oplus{\mathcal{L}}_{2}^{\otimes-1}.

Thus, we obtain that
Y^{P,\theta}_{P,\theta}
is isomorphic to a version of the Hecke stack for
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
:
it classifies pairs
({\mathcal{L}}_{1},D)
, where
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
is a line bundle and
D
is an effective divisor on
X

of degree
\theta
.

6.5.9.

Thus, we have to show:

(j_{Y})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}}\boxtimes R_{Z_{G}})|_{Y^{P,\theta}_{\geq P,<\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}\text{ is ULA }\Rightarrow(j_{\geq P,<\theta,\leq\theta})_{!}\left((j_{Y})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}}\boxtimes R_{Z_{G}})|_{Y^{P,\theta}_{\geq P,<\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}\right)\text{ is ULA.}

6.5.10.

Recall now (see
[
Sch
, Sect. 6.5.5]
) that
Y^{P,\theta}_{\geq P,\leq\theta}
carries an action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, which contracts its
into
Y^{P,\theta}_{P,\theta}
.

6.5.11.
Example

In the example of
G=SL_{2}
, the above action is the following one. A scalar
c\in{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acts on
the triple

{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{1}\overset{\delta_{1}}{\to}{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\overset{\alpha}{\to}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\overset{\delta_{2}}{\to}{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{2}

by

\delta_{1}\mapsto c^{-1}\cdot\delta_{1},\,\,\delta_{2}\mapsto c^{-1}\cdot\delta_{2},\,\,\alpha\mapsto c^{2}\cdot\alpha.

6.5.12.

Since the object

(j_{Y})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}}\boxtimes R_{Z_{G}})|_{Y^{P}_{\geq P,<\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(Y^{P}_{\geq P,<\theta,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

is
quasi-equivariant
with respect to this
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action (i.e., equivariant after passing to a finite self-isogeny
of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
), the required assertion follows from Proposition
6.2.2
.

∎[Theorem
5.1.3
]

7.
Proof of Theorem
4.4.2

We will give two proofs. The first proof is shorter, but it requires more stringent assumptions on the characteristic of the ground field

{\mathsf{k}}
(see Assumption (*) in Sect.
7.1.3
and Remark
7.1.5
).

7.1.
First proof of Theorem
4.4.2

7.1.1.

Let

\operatorname{exp}^{\boxtimes I}/T\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T)

be as in
[
GLC1
, Sect. 3.3]
, where
I
is the Dynkin diagram of
G
(see also
Sect.
8.4.11
below).

Recall that we have a canonically defined
T
-equivariant map

\chi^{I}:\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\to{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I},

and consider the corresponding map

\chi^{I}/T:\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T\to{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T.

Denote

\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I}}/T:=(\chi^{I}/T)^{*}(\operatorname{exp}^{\boxtimes I}/T)\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T).

Recall the map

{\mathsf{p}}:\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

and note that it naturally factors via a map

{\mathsf{p}}/T:\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}.

Recall that the object
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!}
is defined as

({\mathsf{p}}/T)_{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I}}/T)\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

7.1.2.

We consider the above objects over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
. The object
\operatorname{exp}^{\boxtimes I}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}/T
is ULA over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})

(e.g., by Proposition
6.2.2
). Since the map
\chi^{I}/T
is smooth, we obtain that

\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I},{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}/T\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X}),{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}/T)

is also ULA.

For the rest of the first proof, we will consider separately the cases of
g\geq 2
,
g=1
and
g=0
.

7.1.3.

We will make the following assumption on the pair
(G,\operatorname{char}(p))
:

(*)
For every standard Levi
M
and a central character
\check{\mu}
of
Z_{M}
, the
direct summand
({\mathfrak{n}}_{P})_{\check{\mu}}
, viewed as a representation of
M
, has
strangeness

0

(see
[
DG1
, Definition 10.3.4]
for what this means).

Remark 7.1.4
.

As was explained in
loc. cit.
, the above assumption is automatic when one works over a ground
field of characteristic
0
.

Remark 7.1.5
.

According to
[
IMP
, Theorem 3.1]
, the following condition guarantees that Assumption (*) is satisfied:

We need that for all
M
and all roots
\check{\alpha}
appearing in
{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}
,

2\cdot\langle\rho_{M},\check{\alpha}\rangle\cdot|Z^{0}_{M}\cap[M,M]|<p.

In the above formula, the factor
|Z^{0}_{M}\cap[M,M]|
appears since
in
[
IMP
]
only representations with trivial determinant are allowed.

7.1.6.

We proceed with the first proof of Theorem
4.4.2
. We start with the case
g\geq 2
.

Note that the map
{\mathsf{p}}/T
factors as

\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq(2g-2)\cdot\rho)}\overset{\jmath_{(2g-2)\cdot\rho}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G},

where the first arrow is proper (see
[
DG1
, Theorem 7.4.3(a)]
).

Hence, it suffices to show that the functor

(\jmath_{(2g-2)\cdot\rho})_{!}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}^{(\leq(2g-2)\cdot\rho)})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

preserves the property of being ULA over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

We now use Assumption (*). It implies that the complement of
the embedding

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq(2g-2)\cdot\rho)}\overset{\jmath_{(2g-2)\cdot\rho}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

is contractive, see
[
DG1
, Proposition 10.1.3]
.

Hence, the required preservation of the ULA property follows from Proposition
6.2.2
.

7.1.7.

We now consider the case
g=1
. In this case, the map
{\mathsf{p}}/T
factors as

(7.1)

\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq 0)}\overset{\jmath_{0}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}.

Note that
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq 0)}
is the semi-stable locus
\operatorname{Bun}^{ss}_{G}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
. We factor the first arrow in
(
7.1
) as

\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T\overset{\omega\,\text{is trivial}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Bun}_{N}/T\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Bun}^{0}_{B}\to\operatorname{Bun}^{ss}_{G},

where the second arrow is a closed embedding, and
\operatorname{Bun}_{B}^{0}
is the preimage of the neutral connected
component of
\operatorname{Bun}_{T}
under
{\mathsf{q}}:\operatorname{Bun}_{B}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{T}
.

Note that the map

\operatorname{Bun}^{0}_{B}\to\operatorname{Bun}^{ss}_{G}

is proper (indeed,
\operatorname{Bun}_{B}^{0}=\operatorname{Bun}_{B}^{0}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}^{ss}_{G}\to\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{B}^{0}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}^{ss}_{G}
is an equality).

Hence, the first arrow in (
7.1
) is proper. Therefore, it remains to show
that the functor

(\jmath_{0})_{!}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}^{(\leq 0)}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})

preserves the property of being ULA over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

However, in genus 1, the complement of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{(\leq 0)}
in
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is contractive
(by Assumption (*) and
[
DG1
, Proposition 10.1.3]
). Hence, the assertion follows from Proposition
6.2.2
.

7.2.
Proof of Proposition
4.4.2
in genus
0

It remains to consider the case of
g=0
. Here the argument will be of different nature
and in fact can be considered as a simplified version of the second proof relying on special features of the genus 0 situation.

7.2.1.

We will show directly that

\Phi(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})=0

(which is what we need for Property (C)).

However, by Remark
4.1.10
, this is equivalent to the fact that
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
is
ULA over
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

7.2.2.

Note that the direct sum of the constant terms functors

\operatorname{CT}^{\lambda}_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}^{\lambda}_{T}),\quad\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}

is conservative when
g=0
.

Hence, it suffices to show that

\operatorname{CT}^{\lambda}_{*}\circ\Phi(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})=0,\quad\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}.

7.2.3.

Recall also that according to
[
DG2
]
, we have a canonical isomorphism

(7.2)

\operatorname{CT}^{\lambda}_{*}\simeq\operatorname{CT}^{-,\lambda}_{!}.

We claim now that the canonical maps

\operatorname{CT}^{-,\lambda}_{!}\circ\Phi\to\Phi\circ\operatorname{CT}^{-,\lambda}_{!}

are isomorphisms when
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}
.

Indeed, the functor
\operatorname{CT}^{-,\lambda}_{!}
is given by

({\mathsf{q}}^{-})_{!}\circ({\mathsf{p}}^{-})^{*}

for the diagram

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\overset{{\mathsf{p}}^{-}}{\leftarrow}\operatorname{Bun}^{\lambda}_{B^{-}}\overset{{\mathsf{q}}^{-}}{\to}\operatorname{Bun}_{T}^{\lambda}.

Now, for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}
, the map
{\mathsf{p}}^{-}
is smooth, and hence the functor
({\mathsf{p}}^{-})^{*}

commutes with vanishing cycles. The functor
({\mathsf{q}}^{-})_{!}
commutes with vanishing cycles thanks to the
contraction principle (see
[
DG2
, Sect. 4.1]
) and isomorphism (
5.2
).

7.2.4.

Thus, it suffices to show that

\Phi\circ\operatorname{CT}^{-,\lambda}_{!}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})=0.

However, this is the assertion of (
7.6
) below.

∎[Theorem
4.4.2
]

7.3.
Second proof of Theorem
4.4.2

It is easy to reduce the assertion to the case
when
G
is semi-simple, so we will make this assumption.

7.3.1.

Recall (see Remark
4.1.10
) that we have to show that

(7.3)

\Phi(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})=0.

7.3.2.

Recall that we have the semi-orthogonal decomposition of the category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{{\operatorname{Eis}}}\overset{{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{Eis}}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}}{\hookleftarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}.

Thus, every object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
fits into a (canonically defined) fiber sequence

{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{Eis}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{Eis}}^{R}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{\operatorname{cusp}}({\mathcal{F}}).

Furthermore,

{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{Eis}}^{R}({\mathcal{F}})=0\text{ if and only if }\operatorname{CT}_{*}({\mathcal{F}})=0\text{ for all proper parabolics }P\subset G.

7.3.3.

We will show that

(7.4)

{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{\operatorname{cusp}}(\Phi(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}))=0

and

(7.5)

\operatorname{CT}_{*}(\Phi(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}))=0.

By the above, this will imply (
7.3
).

7.4.
The Eisenstein part

We first tackle (
7.5
).

7.4.1.

First, we claim:

Proposition 7.4.2
.

The natural transformation

\Psi\circ\operatorname{CT}_{*}\to\operatorname{CT}_{*}\circ\Psi

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Using Beilinson’s definition of nearby cycles, the functor
\Psi
is the colimit
of functors

{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}\circ({\mathbf{j}}_{0})_{*}((-)\otimes{\mathcal{E}}),

where:

•

The maps
{\mathbf{i}}_{0}
and
{\mathbf{j}}_{0}
are
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{k}})\to\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
and
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{K}}_{0})\to\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})

and base changes thereof;

•

{\mathcal{E}}
is a local system on
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{K}}_{0})
or a pullback thereof.

We claim that the operation
\operatorname{CT}_{*}
commutes with all the three functors involved:

(i) The commutation with
(-)\otimes{\mathcal{E}}
is obvious;

(ii) The commutation with the functor
({\mathbf{j}}_{0})_{*}
is also obvious, since
\operatorname{CT}_{*}

involves !-pullbacks and *-pushforwards.

(iii) In order to establish the commutation with
{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}
, we apply the
isomorphism (
7.2
), and rewrite
\operatorname{CT}_{*}
as
\operatorname{CT}^{-}_{!}
.
Now again the commutation becomes obvious, since
\operatorname{CT}^{-}_{!}
involves *-pullback
and !-pushforward.

∎

Remark 7.4.3
.

Note that a variation of this argument shows that the natural transformation

\operatorname{Sp}\circ\operatorname{CT}_{*}\to\operatorname{CT}_{*}\circ\operatorname{Sp}

is an isomorphism.

Corollary 7.4.4
.

The natural transformation

\Phi\circ\operatorname{CT}_{*}\to\operatorname{CT}_{*}\circ\Phi

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Follows from the fiber sequence

\Phi\to\Psi\to{\mathbf{i}}^{!},

where the functor
{\mathbf{i}}^{!}
obviously commutes with
\operatorname{CT}_{*}
.

∎

7.4.5.

By Corollary
7.4.4
, it suffices to show that

\Phi\circ\operatorname{CT}_{*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}))=0.

We use again the isomorphism (
7.2
), and we rewrite

\operatorname{CT}_{*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})\simeq\operatorname{CT}^{-}_{!}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}).

Thus, we have to prove:

(7.6)

\Phi\circ\operatorname{CT}^{-}_{!}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})=0.

We claim:

Theorem 7.4.6
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

(\operatorname{transl}_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})})^{*}\circ\operatorname{CT}^{-}_{!}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{G,!})[d]\simeq\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{M,!},

where:

•

\operatorname{transl}_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}
is the automorphism of
\operatorname{Bun}_{M}
given by translation by

\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})\in\operatorname{Bun}_{Z_{M}}
;

•

The integer
d
is as in
[
GLC3
, Corollary 10.1.8]
;

•

The functor
(\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\star(-))
is as in Theorem
9.2.7
.

The assertion of Theorem
7.4.6
is obtained from that of Theorem
9.2.7
proven below
by a duality manipulation (see
[
GLC3
, Corollary 10.1.8]
).

Remark 7.4.7
.

Alternatively, one can prove Theorem
7.4.6
by rerunning the argument of Theorem
9.2.7
.
However, the proof is simpler as here one works with the open Zastava space
\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}

and one does not need any local acyclicity assertions.

7.4.8.

Thus, we have to show that

\Phi(\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{M,!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})=0.

By Proposition
4.2.5
,

\Phi(\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\star\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{M,!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})\simeq\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\star\Phi(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{M,!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}).

Now, by induction on the semi-simple rank, we can assume that
\Phi(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{M,!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})=0
,
and the assertion follows.

7.5.
The cuspidal part

7.5.1.

The statement that we want to prove is that the map

(7.7)

{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{\operatorname{cusp}}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{k}}})\simeq{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})\to{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{\operatorname{cusp}}\circ\Psi(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{K}}_{0}})

is an isomorphism.

Thanks to Theorem
4.4.5
, the functors
{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}
,
{\mathbf{j}}_{0,*}
, and

\Psi
are defined on the cuspidal
category, viewed as a
quotient
of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, i.e., in a way that commutes
with the projection

{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}^{L}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}.

7.5.2.

Recall that
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
was defined as

({\mathsf{p}}/T)_{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I}}/T):=(\chi^{I}/T)^{*}(\operatorname{exp}^{\boxtimes I}/T),

where
\operatorname{exp}^{\boxtimes I}/T
is as in
[
GLC1
, Sect. 3.3]
.

We will now change the notations

\operatorname{exp}^{\boxtimes I}/T\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{exp}_{!}^{\boxtimes I}/T\text{ and }\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I}}/T\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I},!}/T

and we note that there exists another object

\operatorname{exp}_{*}^{\boxtimes I}/T\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T),

see Sect.
8.4.11
.

In addition, there is a canonically defined map

(7.8)

\operatorname{exp}_{!}^{\boxtimes I}/T\to\operatorname{exp}_{*}^{\boxtimes I}/T,

see (
8.8
).

7.5.3.

Denote

\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I},*}/T:=(\chi^{I}/T)^{*}(\operatorname{exp}_{*}^{\boxtimes I}/T).

Let

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{*}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

be the object equal to

({\mathsf{p}}/T)_{*}(\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I},*}/T).

The map (
7.8
) gives rise to a map

(7.9)

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!}\to\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{*}.

We will prove:

Theorem 7.5.4
.

The cone of (
7.9
) belongs to
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}
.

Actually, the proof shows more, namely that for any
{\mathcal{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0}))
, the morphism

(7.10)

{\mathsf{p}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0},!}(\pi_{\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega),{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}^{*}({\mathcal{G}})\overset{*}{\otimes}\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I},!}/T)\to{\mathsf{p}}_{{\mathsf{R}}_{0},*}(\pi_{\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega),{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}^{*}({\mathcal{G}})\overset{*}{\otimes}\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I},*}/T)

has cone lying in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})_{{\operatorname{Eis}}}
;
here
\pi_{\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega),{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}}
is the projection
from
\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega),{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}
to
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{R}}_{0})
.

The proof of Theorem
7.5.4
will be given in Sect.
8
. We now proceed with the
proof of the fact that (
7.7
) is an isomorphism.

7.5.5.

Take
{\mathcal{E}}
to be a lisse sheaf on
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathsf{K}}_{0})
.
We have a commutative diagram

(7.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathbf{j}}_{0,*}({\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{j}}_{0,*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{K}}_{0}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{*,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathbf{j}}_{0,*}({\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{j}}_{0,*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{*,{\mathsf{K}}_{0}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{E}}).}

The vertical arrows have Eisenstein cones
by Theorem
7.5.4
. Moreover, the composition
from upper left to bottom right has Eisenstein cone by the property of (
7.10
)
stated above.
Therefore, each arrow in the
above square becomes an isomorphism after applying

{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{\operatorname{cusp}}
.

Applying
{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}
, we obtain a similar diagram

(7.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}){\overset{*}{\otimes}}{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}\circ{\mathbf{j}}_{0,*}({\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}\circ{\mathbf{j}}_{0,*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{K}}_{0}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{*,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}){\overset{*}{\otimes}}{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}\circ{\mathbf{j}}_{0,*}({\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}\circ{\mathbf{j}}_{0,*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{*,{\mathsf{K}}_{0}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{E}})}

in which again all arrows have Eisenstein
cones. By Beilinson’s construction of
nearby cycles, we can pass to a colimit of such
{\mathcal{E}}
’s
so that the top horizontal arrow in the above diagram
becomes the map

{\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}})={\mathbf{i}}_{0}^{*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{R}}_{0}}){\otimes}\Psi(\mathsf{e}_{\operatorname{Spec}(K_{0})})\to\Psi(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!,{\mathsf{K}}_{0}}),

which we deduce has Eisenstein cone.

8.
Comparison of !- vs *- Poincaré objects

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
7.5.4
. We continue to assume that
G
is semi-simple.

8.1.
The case when there exists the exponential sheaf

Note that we only used Theorem
7.5.4
over
{\mathsf{K}}
and
{\mathsf{R}}
. However, we will first give a proof
over a field of positive characteristic (or for D-modules), since it conveys the intuitive picture.

Remark 8.1.1
.

In the course of the proof, we will see that the cone of (
7.9
) admits
a canonical filtration, whose associated graded can be described explicitly, see Remark

8.2.8
.

.

8.1.2.

We start by rewriting the objects

(8.1)

\operatorname{exp}_{!}^{\boxtimes I}/T\text{ and }\operatorname{exp}_{*}^{\boxtimes I}/T

in terms of the exponential sheaf
21
21

21

The definition of these objects in Sect.
8.4.11
uses
the Kirillov model and hence avoids the exponential sheaf.
.

Namely, we start with

\operatorname{exp}^{\boxtimes I}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I})

(here
I
is the set of vertices of the Dynkin diagram)
and the objects (
8.1
) are its !- and *- direct images, respectively,
along the map

{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}\to{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T.

8.1.3.

Consider the stack

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}.

Let
f
denote the projection

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}\to\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}.

We consider the canonical
T
-action on
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}
, where the action
on the second factor is via

T\to T_{\operatorname{adj}}\overset{\operatorname{simple\,roots}}{\simeq}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{I}.

Let
f/T
denote the projection

(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})/T\to\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}/T.

8.1.4.

Let
{\mathbf{j}}
denote the open embedding

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{I}\hookrightarrow\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}.

and
{\mathbf{j}}/T
the embedding

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}/Z_{G}\simeq(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}{}-0)^{I})/T\hookrightarrow(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})/T.

We can rewrite

(8.2)

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{?}\simeq(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{?}\circ(f/T)_{?}\circ({\mathbf{j}}/T)_{?}\circ(\pi_{Z_{G}})_{?}\circ j_{?}\circ(\chi^{I})^{*}(\operatorname{exp}^{\operatorname{\boxtimes I}}),

where:

•

?
is either
!
or
*
;

•

j
denote the embedding
\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\hookrightarrow\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}
;

•

\pi_{Z_{G}}
denotes the projection
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\to\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}/Z_{G}
.

Note that:

•

The map
\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T
is proper, so
(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{!}\to(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{*}
is an isomorphism;

•

The map
\pi_{Z_{G}}
is finite
22
22

22

Recall that
G
was assumed semi-simple.
,
so
(\pi_{Z_{G}})_{!}\to(\pi_{Z_{G}})_{*}
is an isomorphism;

•

The extension of
(\chi^{I})^{*}(\operatorname{exp}^{\operatorname{\boxtimes I}})
along
j
is clean, so the map

j_{!}\circ(\chi^{I})^{*}(\operatorname{exp}^{\operatorname{\boxtimes I}})\to j_{*}\circ(\chi^{I})^{*}(\operatorname{exp}^{\operatorname{\boxtimes I}})

is an isomorphism.

8.1.5.

We can talk about the full category

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}),

where the “extended Whittaker condition” depends in the point in
({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}
(we think of this
({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}
as the
variety of characters of
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
), see Sect.
8.3.1
.

Remark 8.1.6
.

The notation
\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}
(and the idea thereof) is borrowed from
[
Ga2
, Sect. 8]
, where the

extended Whittaker category
is studied.

8.1.7.

We can also consider the corresponding equivariant version

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})^{T}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})^{T}.

We will prove:

Proposition 8.1.8
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})^{T}
, supported
off
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}{}-0)^{I}
, the object

(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{!}\circ(f/T)_{!}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is Eisenstein.

Proposition 8.1.9
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})
, the map

f_{!}({\mathcal{F}})\to f_{*}({\mathcal{F}})

is an isomorphism.

It is clear that the combination of these two propositions implies that (
7.9
) is Eisenstein.

8.2.
Proof of Proposition
8.1.8

8.2.1.

For a subset
J\subset I
, let

{\mathbf{i}}_{J}:(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})\subset(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})

be the embedding of the corresponding stratum, so that
{\mathbf{i}}_{I}={\mathbf{j}}
.

Denote by

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{part}}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})

and

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{part}}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})^{T}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})^{T}.

the corresponding subcategories, obtained by imposing the Whittaker-type equivariance condition.

Remark 8.2.2
.

The notation
\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{part}}
(and the idea thereof) is borrowed from
[
Ga2
, Sect. 7]
.

8.2.3.
