We will show that the functor

(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{!}\circ(f/T)_{!}\circ({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)_{!}:\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{part}}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})^{T}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

factors through the subcategory generated by

{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

where
P
is the standard parabolic corresponding to
J
.

8.2.4.

We stratify
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}
by
(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}
,
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}
,

(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\simeq\operatorname{Bun}_{B}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{T}}{\times}X^{(\lambda)},

where
X^{(\lambda)}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{T}
is the Abel-Jacobi map, shifted by
\rho(\omega_{X})
.
Let
i_{\lambda}
denote the corresponding locally closed embedding.

Consider the corresponding subcategories

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{part}}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})\subset\operatorname{Shv}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})

and

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{part}}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})^{T}\subset\operatorname{Shv}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})^{T}.

We will show that the functor

(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{!}\circ(f/T)_{!}\circ({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)_{!}\circ(i_{\lambda}/T)_{!}:\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{part}}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})^{T}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

factors as

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{part}}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J})^{T}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{M}\overset{{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}}{\to}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}.

8.2.5.

Note that the map

\left((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J}\right)/T\overset{(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)\circ(f/T)\circ({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)\circ(i_{\lambda}/T)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

factors as

\left((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J}\right)/T\overset{f_{P}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{P}\overset{{\mathsf{p}}_{P}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}.

Moreover, we have a Cartesian diagram

\begin{CD}\left((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J}\right)/T\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V\\
\left((\operatorname{Bun}_{B}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{T}}{\times}X^{(\lambda)})\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J}\right)/T@>{{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{q}}_{P}}>{}>\left((\operatorname{Bun}_{B(M)}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{T}}{\times}X^{(\lambda)})\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J}\right)/T\\
@V{f_{P}}V{}V@V{}V{{}^{\prime}\!f_{P}}V\\
\operatorname{Bun}_{P}@>{{\mathsf{q}}_{P}}>{}>\operatorname{Bun}_{M}\\
@V{{\mathsf{p}}_{P}}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\end{CD}

and every object from
\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{part}}\left((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)})_{\lambda}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J}\right)^{T}

is isomorphic to the *-pullback by means of
{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{q}}_{P}
of an object in

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\Bigl((\operatorname{Bun}_{B(M)}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{T}}{\times}X^{(\lambda)})\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{J}\Bigr)/T\right).

8.2.6.

Now,

(8.3)

(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{!}\circ(f/T)_{!}\circ({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)_{!}\circ(i_{\lambda}/T)_{!}\circ({}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{q}}_{P})^{*}\simeq\\
\simeq({\mathsf{p}}_{P})_{!}\circ(f_{P})_{!}\circ({}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{q}}_{P})^{*}\simeq({\mathsf{p}}_{P})_{!}\circ({\mathsf{p}}_{P})^{*}\circ({}^{\prime}\!f_{P})_{!}\simeq{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}\circ({}^{\prime}\!f_{P})_{!}

(up to a cohomological shift
23
23

23

Which is involved in the definition of
{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}
.
), as required.

∎[Proposition
8.1.8
]

Remark 8.2.7
.

We have obtained that the cone of (
7.9
) admits a canonical filtration indexed by
\emptyset\neq J\subset I
,
where the subquotient corresponding to a given
J
in turn has a filtration indexed by
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\operatorname{pos}}
,
and its subquotient corresponding to a given
\lambda
is given by

(8.4)

{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}\Bigl(({}^{\prime}\!f_{P})_{!}\circ({}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{q}}_{P})_{*}\circ(i_{\lambda}/T)^{*}\circ({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)^{*}\circ({\mathbf{j}}/T)_{*}\circ(\pi_{Z_{G}})_{!}\circ j_{!}\circ(\chi^{I})^{*}(\operatorname{exp}^{\operatorname{\boxtimes I}})\Bigr),

(up to a cohomological shift).

One can describe the cone of (
7.9
) more conceptually, by combining the results of
[
Chen
]
and
[
Lin
]
. Namely,
it has a canonical filtration indexed by the poset of proper parabolics with the associated graded corresponding to a given
P

being

{\operatorname{Eis}}_{P}^{\operatorname{enh}}\circ\operatorname{CT}_{P}^{\operatorname{enh}}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!}),

where we refer the reader to
[
Chen
]
for the “enhanced” notation.

Remark 8.2.8
.

The object

({}^{\prime}\!f_{P})_{!}\circ({}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{q}}_{P})_{*}\circ(i_{\lambda}/T)^{*}\circ({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)^{*}\circ({\mathbf{j}}/T)_{*}\circ(\pi_{Z_{G}})_{!}\circ j_{!}\circ(\chi^{I})^{*}(\operatorname{exp}^{\operatorname{\boxtimes I}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})

is closely related to (and can be algorithmically expressed via) the object

({}^{\prime}\!f_{P})_{!}\circ({}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{q}}_{P})_{*}\circ(i_{\lambda}/T)^{!}\circ({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)^{!}\circ({\mathbf{j}}/T)_{*}\circ(\pi_{Z_{G}})_{!}\circ j_{!}\circ(\chi^{I})^{*}(\operatorname{exp}^{\operatorname{\boxtimes I}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M}).

The computation of the latter objects is the main goal of the paper
[
AG2
]
. Namely, it says that this object
identifies with

\operatorname{CT}_{*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!}),

which in turn be calculated using Theorem
7.4.6
.

8.3.
Proof of Proposition
8.1.9

8.3.1.

The proof will fit into the following general paradigm:

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a stack acted on by a vector group
V
. Consider the corresponding category

\operatorname{Whit}(V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

Namely, this is the full subcategory consisting of objects
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Whit}(V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
, equipped with an
isomorphism

(\operatorname{id}\times\operatorname{act})^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq(p_{1,2}^{*}\circ\operatorname{ev}^{*}(\operatorname{exp}))\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{1,3}^{*}({\mathcal{F}})

in
\operatorname{Shv}(V^{*}\times V\times{\mathcal{Y}})
that restricts to the identity map
24
24

24

Since
V
is unipotent as a group,
the full equivariance structure is actually a condition, which is equivalent to the simply-minded one above.

on
V^{*}\times\{0\}\times{\mathcal{Y}}
, where

•

\operatorname{act}
denotes the action map
V\times{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
;

•

\operatorname{ev}
denotes the evaluation map
V^{*}\times V\to{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
.

Note that the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{act}^{*}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(V\times{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{Four}_{Y}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

gives rise to an equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{Whit}(V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

8.3.2.

Let
f
denote the map
V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
. We claim:

Proposition 8.3.3
.

The natural transformation
f_{!}\to f_{*}
becomes an isomorphism when evaluated an objects of
\operatorname{Whit}(V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
.

Proof.

It enough to prove the isomorphism after the (smooth) pullback by means of the map

V\times{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{\operatorname{act}}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}}.

This reduces us to the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}
has the form
V\times{\mathcal{Z}}
with
V
acting on the
first factor.

In this case, the equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}(V\times{\mathcal{Z}})\to\operatorname{Whit}(V^{*}\times V\times{\mathcal{Z}})

is given by

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto p_{1,3}^{*}(\operatorname{Four}_{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathcal{F}}))\overset{*}{\otimes}p^{*}_{1,2}(\operatorname{mult}^{*}(\operatorname{exp})).

The operations

{\mathcal{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Z}})\rightsquigarrow f_{!}\left(p_{1,3}^{*}({\mathcal{G}})\overset{*}{\otimes}p^{*}_{1,2}(\operatorname{mult}^{*}(\operatorname{exp})\right)\text{ and }f_{*}\left(p_{1,3}^{*}({\mathcal{G}})\overset{*}{\otimes}p^{*}_{1,2}(\operatorname{mult}^{*}(\operatorname{exp})\right)

are the !- and *- versions of the functor

\operatorname{Four}_{\mathcal{Z}}:\operatorname{Shv}(V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Z}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(V\times{\mathcal{Z}}).

Now, it is well-know that the natural transformation

\operatorname{Four}_{!,{\mathcal{Z}}}\to\operatorname{Four}_{*,{\mathcal{Z}}}

is an isomorphism.

∎

8.3.4.

We apply the above paradigm as follows. Cover
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega)}
by open substacks

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega),\text{good\,at\,x}},\quad x\in X,

where we require that the generalized
B
-reduction be
non-degenerate
at
x
. It is enough to show
that the map
f_{!}({\mathcal{F}})\to f_{*}({\mathcal{F}})
restricts to an isomorphism over every

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega),\text{good\,at\,x}}.

8.3.5.

Consider the corresponding stack

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}^{\operatorname{level}_{x}}_{N,\rho(\omega),\text{good\,at\,x}},

see
[
Ga3
, Sect. 4.4.2]
.

The stack
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}^{\operatorname{level}_{x}}_{N,\rho(\omega),\text{good\,at\,x}}
is acted on by the group ind-scheme

{\mathfrak{L}}(N_{\rho(\omega)})_{x}
. Let

\chi^{I}_{x}:{\mathfrak{L}}(N_{\rho(\omega)})_{x}\to{\mathbb{G}}^{I}_{a}

denote the canonical character.

8.3.6.

Let
N^{\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{L}}(N_{\rho(\omega)})_{x}
be a sufficiently large subgroup. Let

\overset{\circ}{N}{}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{ker}(\chi^{I}_{x}|_{N^{\prime}}).

Set

{\mathcal{Y}}:=\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}^{\operatorname{level}_{x}}_{N,\rho(\omega),\text{good\,at\,x}}/\overset{\circ}{N}{}^{\prime}.

This is a stack locally of finite type, which carries an action of
V:={\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
. Finally, we note
that the category
\operatorname{Whit}(V^{*}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
identifies with the category

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega),\text{good\,at\,x}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}),

where we think of
({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}
as
V^{*}
.

∎[Proposition
8.1.9
]

8.4.
The Kirillov model

As a preparation to the proof of Theorem
7.5.4
in the setting where the exponential
sheaf does not exist, we discuss the fomalism of
Kirillov
(as opposed to Whittaker)
models.

8.4.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a stack equipped with an action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
. For every subset
J\subset I
, denote by

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{J\operatorname{-cl}}\overset{(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{!}=(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{*}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

the embedding of the full subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{J}}
.

The above functor admits a left and right adjoints, denoted
(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})^{*}
and
(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})^{!}
,
explicitly given by
\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{J}}
and
\operatorname{Av}_{*}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{J}}
, respectively.

8.4.2.

Let
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{J}
be the quotient of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{J\operatorname{-cl}}
obtained by modding out
with respect to all
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{J^{\prime}\operatorname{-cl}}
with
J^{\prime}\supset J
. Denote by
({\mathbf{j}}_{J})^{*}=({\mathbf{j}}_{J})^{!}

the projection

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{J\operatorname{-cl}}\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{J}.

This projection admits left and right adjoints, denoted
({\mathbf{j}}_{J})_{!}
and
({\mathbf{j}}_{J})_{*}
, respectively.

8.4.3.

Denote

({\mathbf{i}}_{J})_{!}:=(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{!}\circ({\mathbf{j}}_{J})_{!}\text{ and }({\mathbf{i}}_{J})_{*}:=(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{*}\circ({\mathbf{j}}_{J})_{*},\quad\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{J}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

These functors admit right and left adjoints, given by

({\mathbf{i}}_{J})^{!}:=({\mathbf{j}}_{J})^{!}\circ(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})^{!}\text{ and }({\mathbf{i}}_{J})^{*}:=({\mathbf{j}}_{J})^{*}\circ(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})^{*},

respectively.

Thus, we obtain a stratification of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
with subquotients
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{J}
.

8.4.4.

In particular, we have the “open stratum” corresponding to
J=\emptyset
.

Note that the essential image of
({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{!}
(resp.,
({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{*}
) consists
of objects for which the !- (resp., *-) averaging for any coordinate copy of
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\subset{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}

vanishes.

On general categorical grounds, we have a natural transformation

(8.5)

({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{!}\to({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{*}.

8.4.5.

Let now
T
be a torus equipped with a surjection onto
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{I}
. Denote

Z_{G}:=\operatorname{ker}(T\to{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{I}).

Assume that
the action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
on
{\mathcal{Y}}
can be extended to an action of
T\ltimes{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
.

Then the discussion in Sects.
8.4.1
-
8.4.3
is applicable to

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T)
. In particular, we obtain the sub/quotient categories

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T)_{J\operatorname{-cl}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T)_{J},

and the corresponding functors

({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)_{!},({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)_{*},({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)^{!},({\mathbf{i}}_{J}/T)^{*},\text{etc.}

8.4.6.

For the next few subsection we will assume that we are in the situation in which the exponential sheaf exists,
and we will explain the connection with the Whittaker picture.

Consider the full subcategories

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})

and

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})^{T}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})^{T},

cf. Sect.
8.1.5
.

Consider the corresponding functors

{\mathbf{j}}_{!}:\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{I})\to\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})

and

({\mathbf{j}}/T)_{!}:\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{I})^{T}\to\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})^{T}.

Recall now that according to Sect.
8.3.1
, we have an equivalence

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

explicitly given by !- (equivalently, *) direct image along the projection
{\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
.

This induces an equivalence

(8.6)

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})^{T}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T).

The following is an elementary verification:

Lemma 8.4.7
.

The equivalence (
8.6
) fits onto the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{I})^{T}@>{({\mathbf{j}}/T)_{!}}>{}>\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I})^{T}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T)_{\emptyset}@>{({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset}/T)_{!}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T).\end{CD}

8.4.8.

Note that using the element
(1,...,1)\in({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{I}
, we can identify

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{I})^{T})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/Z_{G})

Under this identification, the subcategory

\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{I})^{T}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{I})^{T})

corresponds to

\operatorname{Whit}({\mathcal{Y}}/Z_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/Z_{G}).

We claim:

Lemma 8.4.9
.

The equivalence

\operatorname{Whit}({\mathcal{Y}}/Z_{G})\simeq\operatorname{Whit}^{\operatorname{ext}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0)^{I})^{T}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T)_{\emptyset}

is given by the composition

\operatorname{Whit}({\mathcal{Y}}/Z_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/Z_{G})\overset{!-pushforward}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T),

whose image lands in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T)_{\emptyset}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/T)
.

8.4.10.

We now return to the situation when the exponential sheaf does not necessarily exist. We let

{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
, equipped with a natural action of
T\ltimes{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
.

Note that due to monodromicity, the essential image of the embedding

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T)_{\emptyset}\overset{({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{!}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T)

consists of objects whose !-restrictions to

{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{J}\subset{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I},\quad J\neq I

are
0
. I.e., these are objects that are *-extended from
({\mathbb{G}}_{a}-0)^{I}\overset{\jmath}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
.

Similarly, the essential image of the embedding

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T)_{\emptyset}\overset{({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{*}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T)

consists of objects whose *-restrictions to

{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{J}\subset{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I},\quad J\neq I

are
0
. I.e., these are objects that are !-extended from
({\mathbb{G}}_{a}-0)^{I}\overset{\jmath}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
.

We obtain that the category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T)_{\emptyset}
identifies with

\operatorname{Shv}(({\mathbb{G}}_{a}-0)^{I}/T)\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G})

in two different ways
25
25

25

They differ by a cohomological shift.
.

In what follows we will use the identification

(8.7)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T)_{\emptyset}\overset{({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{!}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T)\overset{(\jmath/T)^{*}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(({\mathbb{G}}_{a}-0)^{I}/T)\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G}).

8.4.11.

Denote

\operatorname{exp}_{\emptyset}^{\boxtimes I}/T:=R_{Z_{G}}[-r]\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:ident open}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}/T)_{\emptyset},

where:

•

R_{Z_{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G})
is the !-direct image of
\mathsf{e}\in\operatorname{Vect}=\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})

along
\operatorname{pt}\to\operatorname{pt}/Z_{G}
;

•

r=|I|
is the semi-simple rank of
G
.

In terms of the above identifications, we have

\operatorname{exp}_{!}^{\boxtimes I}/T=({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\emptyset}^{\boxtimes I}/T),

Set

\operatorname{exp}_{*}^{\boxtimes I}/T:=({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{*}(\operatorname{exp}_{\emptyset}^{\boxtimes I}/T).

Thus, explicitly,

\operatorname{exp}_{!}^{\boxtimes I}/T\simeq(\jmath/T)_{*}(R_{Z_{G}})[-r]

and

\operatorname{exp}_{*}^{\boxtimes I}/T\simeq(\jmath/T)_{!}(R_{Z_{G}})[-r].

Note that the natural transformation (
8.5
) gives rise to a map

(8.8)

\operatorname{exp}_{!}^{\boxtimes I}/T\to\operatorname{exp}_{*}^{\boxtimes I}/T.

In other words, this is a map

(8.9)

(\jmath/T)_{*}(R_{Z_{G}})[-r]\to(\jmath/T)_{!}(R_{Z_{G}})[-r],

note the direction of the arrow!

8.4.12.
Examples

Let us analyze the behavior of the map (
8.9
) explicitly.

.
We consider the pullback of the map (
8.9
) to
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}
itself. Thus,
we are dealing with the map

(8.10)

\jmath_{*}(\pi_{!}(\mathsf{e}_{T}))\to\jmath_{!}(\pi_{!}(\mathsf{e}_{T})),

where
\pi:T\to{\mathbb{G}}^{I}_{m}
.

Assume first that
T\to{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{I}
is an isomorphism. Then both sides of (
8.10
),
shifted cohomologically by
[r]
, are perverse, and the cosocle (resp., socle) of

\jmath_{*}(\mathsf{e}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{I}})[r]
(resp., of
\jmath_{!}(\mathsf{e}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{I}})[r]
) is the
\delta
-function at
0
,
i.e.,
\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{pt}}
.

With these identifications, the map (
8.9
) is

\jmath_{*}(\mathsf{e}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{I}})[r]\to\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{pt}}\to\jmath_{*}(\mathsf{e}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{I}})[r].

In the general case, both sides in (
8.10
) have additional
direct factors, given by Kummer sheaves corresponding to non-zero characters of (the finite group)
Z_{G}
. The map
(
8.10
) is the natural isomorphism on these factors.

8.5.
The case when there is no exponential sheaf

In this subsection we will treat the case of Theorem
7.5.4
when we work either over a field
of characteristic
0
or in mixed characteristic.

8.5.1.

We adapt the formalism of Sect.
8.4
to

\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}
, using the method of Sects.
8.3.4
-
8.3.6
.

Thus, we obtain a stratification of the category
\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})
(resp.,
\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T)
)
by sub/quotient categories
\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})_{J}
(resp.,
\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T)_{J}
),

J\subset I
, and similarly for
\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}

8.5.2.

Consider the object

\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I},\emptyset}/T:=(\chi^{I}/T)^{*}(\operatorname{exp}_{\emptyset}^{\boxtimes I}/T)\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T)_{\emptyset}.

First, we note:

Lemma 8.5.3
.

For
J\neq 0
, the projection

\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})\overset{({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})^{*}=({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})^{!}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})_{\emptyset}

annihilates objects supported on
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}-\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}
.

Hence, we obtain that
\operatorname{exp}_{\chi^{I},\emptyset}/T
uniquely extends to an object of

\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T)_{\emptyset}
; we denote it by

\overline{\operatorname{exp}}_{\chi^{I},\emptyset}/T.

8.5.4.

Unwinding the constructions, we obtain that the objects
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{!}
and
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac}}_{*}
are

(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{!}\circ({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{!}(\overline{\operatorname{exp}}_{\chi^{I},\emptyset}/T)\text{ and }(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{*}\circ({\mathbf{j}}_{\emptyset})_{*}(\overline{\operatorname{exp}}_{\chi^{I},\emptyset}/T),

respectively, where
(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{!}\simeq(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{*}
, since the map
\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}
is proper.

Moreover, the map (
7.9
) is induced by the natural transformation
(
8.5
).

Hence, in order to prove Theorem
7.5.4
, it suffices to show that for
J\neq\emptyset
, the essential image of the functor

(8.11)

\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T)_{J\operatorname{-cl}}\overset{(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{!}=(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T)\overset{(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

lies in the essential image of the functor

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\overset{{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

where
P\twoheadrightarrow M
are the standard Levi and parabolic corresponding to
J
.

This is done by a manipulation similar to the one used in the proof of Proposition
8.1.8
.
We elaborate on this below.

8.5.5.

We stratify
\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}
by

(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})_{\lambda}\overset{i_{\lambda}}{\hookrightarrow}\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})},

and consider the corresponding categories

\operatorname{Shv}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})_{\lambda}/T)_{J\operatorname{-cl}}\overset{(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{!}=(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})_{\lambda}/T).

We will show that the composition

(8.12)

\operatorname{Shv}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})_{\lambda}/T)_{J\operatorname{-cl}}\overset{(i_{\lambda})_{!}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T)_{J\operatorname{-cl}}\overset{(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{!}=(\overline{{\mathbf{i}}}_{J})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\operatorname{Shv}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}/T)\overset{(\overline{{\mathsf{p}}}/T)_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

factors as

\operatorname{Shv}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})_{\lambda}/T)_{J\operatorname{-cl}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\overset{{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

where
P
is the standard parabolic corresponding to
J\subset I
.

This follows from the Cartesian diagram

\begin{CD}(\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})_{\lambda}/T\\
@V{\sim}V{}V\\
(\operatorname{Bun}_{B}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{T}}{\times}X^{(\lambda)})/T@>{{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{q}}_{P}}>{}>(\operatorname{Bun}_{B(M)}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{T}}{\times}X^{(\lambda)})/T\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Bun}_{P}@>{{\mathsf{q}}_{P}}>{}>\operatorname{Bun}_{M}\\
@V{{\mathsf{p}}_{P}}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\end{CD}

using the following observation:

Lemma 8.5.6
.

The functor of *-pullback along
{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{q}}_{P}
gives rise to an equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}((\operatorname{Bun}_{B(M)}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{T}}{\times}X^{(\lambda)})/T)\to\operatorname{Shv}((\overline{\operatorname{Bun}}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})})_{\lambda}/T)_{J\operatorname{-cl}}.

∎[Theorem
7.5.4
]

9.
Langlands functor and Eisenstein series

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
1.4.6
.

Remark 9.0.1
.

The proof that we will give applies in any sheaf-theoretic context (e.g., de Rham and Betti). We note, however,
that the construction of the isomorphism of functors stated in Theorem
1.4.6

is a priori different from that in
[
GLC3
, Theorem 14.2.2]
.

The reason that we give a (different) proof here is that some ingredients of the proof in
[
GLC3
]

(specifically, the semi-infinite geometric Satake) have only been developed in the de Rham context.

It is a good (but potentially quite involved) exercise to show that the two isomorphisms actually
agree.

9.1.
Reducing to a statement about
{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}

This step is parallel to
[
GLC3
, Sect. 14.2.3]
.

9.1.1.

Denote

{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}_{\operatorname{coarse}}:=\Upsilon^{\vee}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}\circ{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}_{\operatorname{coarse}}\circ\Xi_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}}},\quad{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}).

In other words,

{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}_{\operatorname{coarse}}=({\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}})_{*}\circ({\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}})^{*};

note that map
{\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}
is schematic, so the functor
({\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}})_{*}
is well-behaved.

9.1.2.

We claim that in order to construct the datum of commutativity for the diagram (
1.6
), it is enough to
do so for the diagram

(9.1)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})@>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{M,\operatorname{coarse}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})\\
@V{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}[\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}]}V{}V@V{}V{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}_{\operatorname{coarse}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{}>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

Indeed, to prove the commutativity of (
1.6
), it is enough to show
that the two circuits are isomorphic when evaluated on compact objects of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Note that diagram (
9.1
) is obtained from diagram (
1.6
) by composing both
circuits with

\Upsilon^{\vee}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}).

Since the functor
\Upsilon^{\vee}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}}
is fully faithful on the eventually coconnective subcategory,
it suffices to show that both circuits in (
1.6
) send compact objects in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

to eventually coconnective objects in
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
.

9.1.3.

We first show this for the anti-clockwise circuit.

The functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}
preserves compactness, and hence so does its translated version

{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}
. Hence, the required assertion follows from Theorem
1.1.10
.

9.1.4.

We now consider the clockwise circuit. The top horizontal arrow sends compact objects to eventually coconnective objects
again by Theorem
1.1.10
(applied to
M
).

Finally, the functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}_{\operatorname{coarse}}
has a bounded cohomological amplitude on the left,
since the morphism
{\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}
is quasi-smooth.

9.2.
Method of proof

The proof will be a geometric counterpart of the computation of Whittaker coefficients of
Eisenstein series, coupled with (the parabolic version of) the theory developed in
[
BG2
]
and
[
FH
]
.

9.2.1.

Recall that the functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}
extends to a
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear functor

(9.2)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

see the proof of Proposition
1.4.2
. We will denote the functor in (
9.2
) by
{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}^{-,\operatorname{part.enh}}
.

We will denote by
{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}^{-,\operatorname{part.enh}}
the precomposition of
{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}^{-,\operatorname{part.enh}}

with the corresponding translation functor (this is well-defined as the action of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})

on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})
commutes with central translations).

Remark 9.2.2
.

The functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}^{-,\operatorname{part.enh}}
corresponds under the Langlands functor
to the functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{spec,part.enh}}
studied in
[
GLC3
, Sect. 12.6-7]
.

9.2.3.

In order to construct the commutativity datum for (
9.1
), it suffices to do so for the diagram

(9.3)

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})@>{\operatorname{Id}\otimes{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{M,\operatorname{coarse}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}})\\
@V{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{part.enh}}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}[\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}]}V{}V@V{}V{({\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}})_{*}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{}>{{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{G,\operatorname{coarse}}}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

Since both circuits in (
9.3
) are
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}})
-linear, in order to construct
the commutativity datum for (
9.3
), it is enough to do so for its composition with the
functor
\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}},-)
.

Thus, we need to construct the datum of commutativity for the diagram

(9.4)

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})@>{\operatorname{Id}\otimes{\mathbb{L}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{M,\operatorname{coarse}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}})\\
@V{{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-,\operatorname{part.enh}}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}[\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}]}V{}V@V{}V{\Gamma_{!}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}},-)}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{}>{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}^{\operatorname{Vac}}}>\operatorname{Vect}.\end{CD}

9.2.4.

Denote

\Omega^{\operatorname{glob}}:={\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}}})\in\operatorname{ComAlg}({\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})).

The morphism
{\mathsf{q}}^{-,\operatorname{spec}}
is “as good as affine” (see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 12.7.5]
for what this means). Hence, we
can identify

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{{\check{P}}^{-}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\simeq\Omega^{\operatorname{glob}}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})).

The datum of the functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}_{!}^{-,\operatorname{part.enh}}
can be interpreted as the action of
\Omega^{\operatorname{glob}}
,
viewed as a monad on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})
, on the functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}
.

Thus, the datum of commutativity of (
9.4
) can be stated as an isomorphism of functors

(9.5)

\operatorname{coeff}_{G}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\circ{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}[\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}]\simeq\operatorname{coeff}_{M}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\circ(\Omega^{\operatorname{glob}}\star-)

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\to\operatorname{Vect},

acted on by the monad
\Omega^{\operatorname{glob}}
. In the above formula,
(-)\star(-)
denotes the action of

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})
on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})
.

In what follows we will construct an isomorphism (
9.5
); its compatibility with the action
of
\Omega^{\operatorname{glob}}
would follow by unwinding the construction of
[
BG2
]

and
[
FH
]
26
26

26

More precisely, this follows by combining the Koszul duality statement in the proof of
Corollary 6.4.1.2 with the proof of Proposition 4.5.4.1 in
[
FH
]
.
, see Remark
9.3.13
.

9.2.5.

Recall the category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{M}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, which acts on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})
. Its action of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})

factors through the localization functor

\operatorname{Loc}_{\check{M}}^{\operatorname{spec,restr}}:{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{M}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}}),

see
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 12.7.1]
.

Let
\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{M}})
be the commutative algebra

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\operatorname{Chev}}({\check{{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{-}_{P}).

We attach to it the commutative factorization algebra

\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{M}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

which we view as a (commutative) algebra object with respect to the (symmetric) monoidal
structure on
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{M}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, see
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.10.4]
.

By
[
GLC3
, Sect. 12.3.5]
, we have

\Omega^{\operatorname{glob}}\simeq\operatorname{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{spec,restr}}(\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})),

as commutative algebra objects in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{M}})
.

9.2.6.

We can view
\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})
as a monad acting on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})
. We will prove
the following generalization of (
9.5
):

Theorem 9.2.7
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{coeff}_{G}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\circ{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}[\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}]\simeq\operatorname{coeff}_{M}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\circ(\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\star(-))

as functors
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\to\operatorname{Vect}
, where
(-)\star(-)
denotes the monoidal action of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{M}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Remark 9.2.8
.

One can view formula (
9.5
) as a geometric counterpart of the classical
computation of the Whittaker coefficient of Eisenstein series.

The latter says that
the Whittaker coefficient of Eisenstein series of an automorphic function on
M

equals the Whittaker coefficient of a particular Hecke operator applied to that
automorphic function.

The Hecke functor
\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})
is the geometric counterpart of
that classical Hecke functor.

Remark 9.2.9
.

Theorem
9.2.7
is equivalent to a (particular case of)
[
GLC3
, Corollary 10.1.5]
,
and its proof is parallel to that of
[
GLC3
, Theorem 10.1.2]
in Sect. 10.3 of
loc. cit.

The essential difference
27
27

27

Alluded to in Remark
9.0.1
.
, however, is
that our key computational ingredient here is Proposition
9.3.12
, whereas in
[
GLC3
]

it is Proposition 10.6.8 of
loc. cit.
, which uses the local theory developed in that paper,
specifically Corollary 2.5.2 in
loc. cit.
.

9.3.
Proof of Theorem
9.2.7

We will give a proof when
\operatorname{char}(k)
is positive, i.e., when the Artin-Schreier sheaf exists;
the proof in the characteristic zero case is completely parallel: one replaces the Artin-Schreier sheaf
by the procedure of
[
GLC1
, Sect. 3.3]
.

9.3.1.

Denote

\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}:=\operatorname{Bun}_{B}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{T}}{\times}\{\rho(\omega_{X})\},

and denote by

\chi:\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\to{\mathbb{G}}_{a}

that map

\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\overset{\chi^{I}}{\to}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{I}\overset{\operatorname{sum}}{\to}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}.

Let
{\mathsf{p}}
denote the projection

\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}.

Recall that the functor
\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{Vac}}
is by defintion

(9.6)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})},{\mathsf{p}}^{!}(-)\overset{!}{\otimes}\chi^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega})),

where
\operatorname{exp}_{\omega}
denotes the Artin-Schreier sheaf on
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
, normalized so that
it behaves multiplicatively with respect to the !-pullback.

9.3.2.

Recall that the functor
{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}
is defined by

(9.7)

({\mathsf{p}}^{-})_{!}\circ({\mathsf{q}}^{-})^{*}(-)[\operatorname{dim.rel}],

where:

•

{\mathsf{p}}^{-}:\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
;

•

{\mathsf{q}}^{-}:\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{M}
;

•

\operatorname{dim.rel}
is the dimension of
\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}
over
\operatorname{Bun}_{M}
(it depends on the connected
component of
\operatorname{Bun}_{M}
).

9.3.3.

Let

\begin{CD}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}@<{j}<{}<\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}\\
@V{\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-}}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\end{CD}

denote Drinfeld’s relative compactification of
\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}
along
{\mathsf{p}}^{-}
.
Denote by
\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-}
the map
\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{M}
.

Note that the ULA property of the object
j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})

with respect to
\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-}
(see
[
BG1
, Theorem 5.1.5]
) implies that the natural transformation

j_{!}\circ(\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-})^{*}(-)\simeq j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}})\overset{*}{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-})^{*}(-)\to j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}})\overset{!}{\otimes}(\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-})^{!}(-)[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})]

is an isomorphism.

Hence, we can rewrite

(9.8)

{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}(-)\simeq(\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-})_{*}\left((\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-})^{!}(-)\overset{!}{\otimes}j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}})\right)[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})+\operatorname{dim.rel}].

9.3.4.

Consider the diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}}
\scriptstyle{{}^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-}}
\scriptstyle{{}^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-}}

By base change and projection formula, we obtain:

(9.9)

\operatorname{coeff}_{G}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\circ{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!}(-)\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}\left(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}},\bigl((\,^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}})^{!}\circ((\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-})^{!}(-)\overset{!}{\otimes}j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}}))\bigr)\overset{!}{\otimes}\left((\,^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-})^{!}\circ\chi^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega})\right)\right)\\
[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})+\operatorname{dim.rel}].

9.3.5.

Let

(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}

be the open substack corresponding to the condition that the
N
-reduction and the generalized
P^{-}
-reduction of the G-bundle are transversal
at the generic point of the curve:

\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}}
\textstyle{(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}}}
\scriptstyle{{}^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-}}
\scriptstyle{{}^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\,{}^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{tr}}}
\scriptstyle{\,{}^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}}^{\operatorname{tr}}}

As in
[
GLC3
, Lemma 10.6.3]
, we have:

Lemma 9.3.6
.

The natural transformation

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}\left(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}},\bigl((\,^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}})^{!}\circ((\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-})^{!}(-)\overset{!}{\otimes}j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}}))\bigr)\overset{!}{\otimes}\left((\,^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-})^{!}\circ\chi^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega})\right)\right)\to\\
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}\left((\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}},\bigl((\,^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}}^{\operatorname{tr}})^{!}\circ((\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-})^{!}(-)\overset{!}{\otimes}j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}}))\bigr)\overset{!}{\otimes}\left((\,^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{tr}})^{!}\circ\chi^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega})\right)\right)

is an isomorphism.

Hence, we obtain that the expression in (
9.9
) can be rewritten as

(9.10)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}\left((\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}},\bigl((\,^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}}^{\operatorname{tr}})^{!}\circ((\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-})^{!}(-)\overset{!}{\otimes}j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}}))\bigr)\overset{!}{\otimes}\left((\,^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{tr}})^{!}\circ\chi^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega})\right)\right)\\
[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})+\operatorname{dim.rel}].

9.3.7.

Recall the (parabolic) Zastava space

\operatorname{Zast}:=(\operatorname{Bun}_{P}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}},

which is the open substack of
\operatorname{Bun}_{P}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}
, corresponding to
the condition that the
P
-reduction and the generalized
P^{-}
-reduction are transversal
at the generic point of the curve, see
[
BFGM
, Sect. 2.2 and Proposition 3.2]
.

The stack
\operatorname{Zast}
is endowed with a map

{\mathfrak{s}}:\operatorname{Zast}\to\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}},

where:

•

{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}
is the sheafification of the Ran space in the topology of finite surjective maps;

•

\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}
is the corresponding version of the Hecke stack for
M
;

Remark 9.3.8
.

In the formulation in
[
BFGM
]
, the map
{\mathfrak{s}}
rather goes to a version of
\operatorname{Hecke}(M)
over
the space of colored divisors on
X
, which is the union of schemes of the form

X^{(\underline{n})}:=\underset{i}{\Pi}\,X^{(n_{i})},\quad n_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0},

where
i
runs over (a subset of) the Dynkin diagram of
G
.

There is no map from
X^{(\underline{n})}
to
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, however, there is one to
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}
; namely it comes
from the map

X^{\underline{n}}:=\underset{i}{\Pi}\,X^{n_{i}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}},

which is invariant with respect to

\Sigma_{\underline{n}}:=\underset{i}{\Pi}\,\Sigma_{n_{i}},

where
\Sigma_{n}
is the symmetric group on
n
letters.

9.3.9.

Note that the stack
(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}}
that appears
in (
9.10
) is canonically isomorphic to

\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M}}{\times}\operatorname{Zast},

where:

•

N(M)
is the maximal unipotent subgroup of the Levi
M
;

•

The map
\operatorname{Zast}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{M}
is the composition

\operatorname{Zast}\overset{{\mathfrak{s}}}{\to}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}\overset{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\to}\operatorname{Bun}_{M}.

Under this identification, the map

(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}}\overset{\,{}^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}}^{\operatorname{tr}}}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}\overset{\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}^{-}}{\to}\operatorname{Bun}_{M}

corresponds to

\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M}}{\times}\operatorname{Zast}\overset{\operatorname{id}\times{\mathfrak{s}}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}\overset{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}}{\to}\operatorname{Bun}_{M}.

9.3.10.

Let us denote by

\Omega_{\chi}\in\operatorname{Shv}\Bigl(\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}\Bigr)

the object equal to the *-direct image along

(\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}}\simeq\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M}}{\times}\operatorname{Zast}\overset{\operatorname{id}\times{\mathfrak{s}}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}

of

\left((\,^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}}^{\operatorname{tr}})^{!}\circ j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}})\right)\overset{!}{\otimes}\left((\,^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{tr}})^{!}\circ\chi^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega})\right)\in\operatorname{Shv}\Bigl((\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}}\Bigr).

Let us denote by
r_{2}
the projection

\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}.

Applying the projection formula, we obtain that the expression in (
9.10
) identifies with

(9.11)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}\left(\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}},(r_{2}^{!}\circ\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{!}(-))\overset{!}{\otimes}\Omega_{\chi}\right)\\
[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})+\operatorname{dim.rel}].

9.3.11.

Consider the map

(9.12)

\operatorname{transl}_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}:\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}\to\\
\to\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}},

given by (central) translation by
\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})
.

Let
r_{1}
denote the projection

\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})}.

Here is the key computational input in the proof of Theorem
1.4.6
:

Proposition 9.3.12
.

The pullback of
\Omega_{\chi}
along (
9.12
), viewed as an object of

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}),

shifted cohomologically by

[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})+\operatorname{dim.rel}+\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}],

identifies canonically with

(r_{1}^{!}\circ\chi_{M}^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega}))\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{M}\circ\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})),

where:

•

\chi_{M}:\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})}\to{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
is the counterpart of the map
\chi
for
M
.

•

\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{M}:{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{M}})\to\operatorname{Sph}_{M}
is the naive geometric Satake functor;

•

By a slight abuse of notation, we denote by
\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{M}\circ\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}}))
the image
of the corresponding object under the equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}).

Remark 9.3.13
.

Recall that in order to deduce Theorem
1.4.6
from Theorem
9.2.7
we also need to know
that the isomorphism (
9.5
) is compatible with
\Omega^{\operatorname{glob}}
-actions.

This compatibility follows from the corresponding property of the isomorphism of Proposition
9.3.12
.

9.3.14.

Let us assume Proposition
9.3.12
for a moment and finish the proof of Theorem
9.2.7
.

Indeed, combining (
9.11
) with Proposition
9.3.12
, we obtain that the functor

\operatorname{coeff}_{G}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\circ{\operatorname{Eis}}^{-}_{!,\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}[\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}]

is isomorphic to

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}\Bigl(\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}},(r_{2}^{!}\circ\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{!}(-))\overset{!}{\otimes}(r_{1}^{!}\circ\chi_{M}^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega}))\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{M}\circ\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}}))\Bigr)

By base change and projection formula, the letter expression identifies with

(9.13)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}\left(\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})},{\mathsf{p}}_{M}^{!}\Bigl(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}_{*}\bigl(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{!}(-)\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{M}\circ\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}}))\bigr)\Bigr)\overset{!}{\otimes}\chi_{M}^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega})\right),

where

{\mathsf{p}}_{M}:\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{M}.

We have, by definition:

\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}_{*}\left(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}^{!}(-)\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{M}\circ\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega))\right)\simeq\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\star(-),

as endofunctors of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Thus, the expression in (
9.13
) identifies with

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}\left(\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})},{\mathsf{p}}_{M}^{!}\left(\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\star(-)\right)\overset{!}{\otimes}\chi_{M}^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega})\right)\simeq\operatorname{coeff}_{M}^{\operatorname{Vac}}\left(\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})\star(-)\right),

as required.

∎[Theorem
9.2.7
]

9.4.
Proof of Proposition
9.3.12

9.4.1.

Note that the map
\chi
is a sum of two maps
\chi^{M}
and
\chi^{N_{P}}
, where the former
is the sum over the simple roots in the Dynkin diagram of
M
and the latter
is the sum over the other simple roots.

Note that the composition

\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M}}{\times}\operatorname{Zast}\simeq\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}\overset{{}^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{tr}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{N,\rho(\omega_{X})}\overset{\chi^{M}}{\to}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}

identifies with

\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M}}{\times}\operatorname{Zast}\overset{r_{1}}{\to}\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\overset{\chi_{M}}{\to}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}.

Denote by
\Omega_{\chi^{N_{P}}}
the object of
\operatorname{Shv}\Bigl(\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}\Bigr)

defined in the same way as
\Omega_{\chi}
, but with
\chi
replaced by
\chi_{N^{P}}
.

By base change Proposition
9.3.12
is equivalent to the isomorphism

(9.14)

(\operatorname{transl}_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})})^{!}(\Omega_{\chi^{N_{P}}})[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})+\operatorname{dim.rel}+\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}]\simeq\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{M}\circ\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})

as objects in

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{N(M),\rho_{M}(\omega_{X})}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{M},\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\times}\operatorname{Hecke}(M)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\prime}}).

9.4.2.

Let

\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}\overset{j_{\operatorname{Zast}}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Zast}

be the open Zastava, i.e., the corresponding open

(\operatorname{Bun}_{P}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}})^{\operatorname{tr}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{P}\underset{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}.

By a slight abuse of notation, let us denote by
{}^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}}^{\operatorname{tr}}
the map

\operatorname{Zast}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}_{P^{-}}
and by
{}^{\prime}\overset{\circ}{{\mathsf{p}}}{}^{\operatorname{tr}}
the map

\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}.

Since the stacks below involved are smooth, we have

(\,^{\prime}\overset{\circ}{{\mathsf{p}}}{}^{\operatorname{tr}})^{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}})\simeq\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}}[2(\dim(\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}})-\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}))].

From here we obtain a map

(9.15)

(j_{\operatorname{Zast}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}})[2(\dim(\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}})-\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}))]\to(\,^{\prime}{\mathsf{p}}^{\operatorname{tr}})^{!}\circ j_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{P^{-}}}).

The next assertion is
[
Lin
, Lemma 4.1.10]
:

Lemma 9.4.3
.

The map (
9.15
) is an isomorphism.

9.4.4.

From Lemma
9.4.3
, we obtain that the object

\Omega_{\chi^{N_{P}}}[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})+\operatorname{dim.rel}+\delta_{(N^{-}_{P})_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}}]

is isomorphic to

(9.16)

(\operatorname{id}\times{\mathfrak{s}})_{*}\left(\Bigl(r_{2}^{!}\circ(j_{\operatorname{Zast}})_{!}(\underline{\mathsf{e}}_{\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}}})\Bigr)\overset{!}{\otimes}\Bigl((\,\widetilde{\mathsf{p}}^{-,\operatorname{tr}})^{!}\circ(\chi^{N_{P}})^{!}(\operatorname{exp}_{\omega})\Bigr)\right)[\dim(\overset{\circ}{\operatorname{Zast}})].

The required isomorphism between the pullback of (
9.16
) along
\operatorname{transl}_{\rho_{P}(\omega_{X})}

and the object
\operatorname{Sat}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{M}\circ\operatorname{Fact}(\Omega^{\operatorname{loc}})
follows from
[
Ra1
, Sect. 4.6.1]
(for
P=B
)
and
[
FH
, Theoren 1.4.3.1]
(for an arbitrary parabolic).
